## 2026-07-09 · CI red — actual causes from the Actions logs · blocked-on-founder (PAT rejected → resolved above)
- **Correction of the record (previous entry below):** the Actions job logs show the red runs never reached `pnpm install` — every run through #4 died in seconds at `pnpm/action-setup@v4`: *"Error: Multiple versions of pnpm specified: version 10 in the GitHub Action config … version pnpm@10.33.0 in the package.json"*. The anonymous-fetch diagnosis below was therefore not the first cause; the PAT step had never been exercised. Fixed in `0d775e3` (drop the action's `version` input; `packageManager` is the single source).
- **Run #5 (`0d775e3`, run id 29040910501) reached the real fetch and surfaced the second layer:** the auth step ran, pnpm resolved 631 packages, then `git fetch --depth 1 origin b10f4822…` failed with **`remote: Invalid username or token. Password authentication is not supported for Git operations.`** A credential WAS presented (anonymous would read "could not read Username", as in the local repro) — **GitHub rejected the token in `PLATFORM_CONTRACTS_READ_TOKEN`.** Blocked on founder: verify the fine-grained PAT (repository access explicitly includes `beurni2/platform-contracts` · Repository permissions → Contents: Read-only · not expired · value pasted exactly).
- `workflow_dispatch:` added to `ci.yml` so the run can be retried from the Actions UI (or by the CTO session) after the secret is fixed — no push needed.

## 2026-07-09 · CI infra patch — red Actions runs on main · done (founder-directed; superseded by the correction above)
- **Cause (diagnosed with evidence before changing anything):** both Actions runs on `main` died in ~8–9s at `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` — the only step that fetches `platform-contracts` (all four `@platform/*` git deps + the workspace override point at `https://github.com/beurni2/platform-contracts.git`). The canon repo is private; the GitHub runner fetches anonymously. Reproduced locally under the runner's condition (`GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 git ls-remote …`): `fatal: could not read Username for 'https://github.com': terminal prompts disabled`, exit 128.
- **Fix (founder ruling: option (b) fine-grained READ-ONLY PAT approved; making the repo public and vendoring rejected):** one CI-only step inserted immediately before the install step — a `git config --global url.…insteadOf` rewrite injecting `secrets.PLATFORM_CONTRACTS_READ_TOKEN` for the `platform-contracts` URL, in the runner's ephemeral git config only. No token material in any committed file, log, or lockfile URL (verified: the secret name appears once, in `ci.yml`, as a `secrets.` reference). Commit `8a1a835` directly on `main` — founder-approved exception for this one commit.
- **Verification:** the proof is the Actions run on `main`, visible only to the founder — founder verifies the run.
- **Follow-up (tracked):** bake this auth step into `platform-contracts/CONSUMING.md` at its next touch, so every future app repo carries it from birth.

## 2026-07-10 · CI incident — ssh/scp git-URL forms vs the runner auth rewrite · fixed on main (founder-approved CI-fix exception)
- **Diagnosis (evidence-first):** origin main red after the WO-1.4 merge. Lockfile grep: every git-dep resolution recorded `repo: git@github.com:beurni2/platform-contracts.git` (pure scp) + hybrid `git+https://git@github.com:…` version keys — while package.json/workspace specifiers stayed https. Root mechanism: this sandbox injects git config VIA ENVIRONMENT (`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`/`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_*`), transparently rewriting ssh↔https beneath file-level isolation (GIT_TRACE log-copied: `git@github.com:…` → `git-remote-https … https://github.com/…`) — which is how pnpm recorded scp forms AND why local installs stayed green. With env config neutralized (`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=0`) and ONLY the ci.yml https rewrite active: the scp form attempted SSH transport (rewrite did NOT match — on a runner: publickey failure), while the https form with a dummy token hit "Invalid username or token" (401-class = form MATCHED). Hypothesis reproduced.
- **Fix (`317a85e`, ci-only commit on main):** ① ci.yml auth step now rewrites ALL THREE forms (https, `ssh://git@github.com/…`, `git@github.com:…`) to tokened https; ② lockfile normalized to https form — 70 changed lines, URL-form only, same sha `9308c641` throughout, zero scp forms remain (diff log-copied). Re-verification: all three forms under the fixed config + dummy token → "Invalid username or token" (form-match PASS ×3); `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` green on the normalized lockfile. No token printed anywhere.
- **Sweep (read-only):** sera pnpm-lock.yaml carries the SAME class — 26 scp-form lines (its journal's "watch at the first PR CI run" item, now confirmed); shop-plus is clean (0 hits). Fixes there follow on the founder's word.
- **The runner's verdict is the only proof that closes this** — founder verifies the new Actions run.

## 2026-07-11 · CI INCIDENT #3 (expo-preview red on its first armed runs) · fixed on main `57a8e47`
- **Every armed `expo-preview` run was red** (boutik ×4, shop ×2, sera ×2). Diagnosis from the complete Actions logs (pulled via API, log-copied): token check, checkout, install, `eas init --non-interactive --force` (`✔ Project successfully linked (ID: 0f9e5eeb-7aaa-4c76-9704-a966e7100699)`) and `update:configure` (`✔ Configured updates.url`) all PASSED — the failure is the third command's bundling: `SyntaxError: index.ts: Cannot find module 'babel-preset-expo'`, identical across the three repos. The token and the founder's account are NOT the cause.
- **Root cause (config-class, reproduced):** `babel.config.js` names `babel-preset-expo`; the app manifest never declared it; pnpm strict isolation leaves it unresolvable from the app directory on a fresh install. Reproduced byte-identically in a cold clone with an isolated Metro cache (exit 1, same SyntaxError); a direct `@babel/core` transform emulation confirms babel has no fallback.
- **Why WO-4.0's evidence missed it (failure-mode #8, named):** the `expo start --offline` boot probe transforms nothing until a client connects, and local exports silently reused Metro's cross-directory content-hash cache (`$TMPDIR/metro-cache`), bypassing babel. Standing lesson: bundling evidence = a cache-isolated `expo export`, never a dev-server boot line.
- **Fix `57a8e47`:** `babel-preset-expo ~13.2.5` (expo 53.0.27's own declared range) added to `apps/supplier-app` devDependencies; lockfile by regeneration (+3 lines, zero new packages, dup-key assert 0). Evidence: resolve-from-app-dir OK · gates exit 0 (ALL GATES GREEN) · cold-clone isolated-cache export exit 0 (678 modules bundled).
- **The founder's next expo-preview runs are the only closure** — this entry claims the local reproduction and fix, never the publish.

## 2026-07-11 · WO-4.2R (LE VISAGE, supplier-app) · in review — branch `e4/wo-4.2r`
- **Founder verdict driving this order, verbatim:** "still trash not looking like real apps. these simple prototypes are even looking better than what you have built." Supersedes WO-4.2 (la capture guidée) — **no WO-4.2 branch existed in any repo; nothing was mid-build, nothing to park** (verified by branch listing before starting).
- **Setup `4070356` + `0a51f0e`:** canon pin **v0.6.0 (`539dbc8a`, the Signature Design Doctrine)** — bumped in every package.json AND pnpm-workspace.yaml overrides (first attempt taught: the overrides own the resolution); /docs +DESIGN-LANGUAGE.md byte-exact (sha256 `e478db70…`); lockfile by regeneration, 0 dup keys; drift args → 0.6.0. The founder's prototype committed VERBATIM under `design-reference/` — **the four order attachments were byte-identical (md5 `ab8dcba6…`); one canonical copy committed**, README states its reference-only status (canon tokens override it everywhere).
- **THE EXTRACTION (the order's first mandate — what the prototype does that reads "real app"):**
  · **Header pattern:** a compact top bar on every screen — back affordance as a BORDERED CHIP (not a bare arrow), single-line title in display type with an optional one-line muted context sub, a right-side status chip. Nothing floats in a void.
  · **Card anatomy:** raised card on a warm ground, hairline border + generous radius + whisper shadow; inside: uppercase letter-spaced OVERLINE → value/heading → muted sub; money blocks are label/value receipt rows with tabular bold values closed by a dashed-rule total.
  · **List-row density:** rows are tiles — leading art/glyph block, 15-16px/700 title + one muted meta line, a semantic status pill right, ~10px gaps. One glance = identity + state + what it pays.
  · **Button hierarchy:** full-width ~52px primary (filled, radius ~15, 700); secondary = soft theme wash + deep text; ghost = bordered; danger = filled red; press = scale(.98) within ~80ms; disabled = faded. ONE primary per screen; everything else whispers.
  · **Spacing rhythm:** 8/12/16/20 stack rhythm, 16px gutters, sections introduced by overlines, scroll content padded so the tab bar never covers it.
  · **What makes it read "real":** (a) chrome everywhere — header + bottom tab bar with soft-pill active state; (b) DENSITY — stat grids, information-rich tiles, never centered prose walls; (c) semantic state color as pills, never sentences; (d) sticky bottom CTAs on wizards; (e) product art treated with respect; (f) tabular numerals on every franc; (g) press feedback and sheet/toast motion; (h) identity + personal context in the header; (i) the woven tri-color band as the ecosystem signature.
  · **Canon notes:** the prototype carries legacy branding and a DARK Séra — direction only; ui-tokens v2 palettes and DESIGN-LANGUAGE.md win (charter §2.5).

## 2026-07-12 · WO-4.2D (capture incident + founder UX round) · in review — branch `e4/wo-4.2d`
- **The incident (founder device, verbatim): « La photo n'a pas pu être prise » on iPhone/Expo Go.** Anchoring: branch off main `8ba0e61` (WO-4.2C merge `083639b` ancestor-verified; local==origin).
- **THE THROW-SITE TABLE (Part A item 1 — every throw on the capture path, `App.tsx takeShot → captureShot`):**
  | # | Site | Trigger | Surfaced as |
  |---|------|---------|-------------|
  | T1 | `camera.takePictureAsync({quality:1})` capture.ts:52 | native: permission revoked mid-session · camera not ready (tap before `onCameraReady`) · preview paused (Android throws) · AVFoundation interruption | generic rejection → chip |
  | T2 | `ImageManipulator.manipulate(uri)` → `renderAsync()` (derivative) capture.ts:37-39 | native: unreadable/expired uri · decode failure · disk | rejection → chip |
  | T3 | `image.saveAsync({compress, format, base64:true})` capture.ts:40 | native: encode failure · disk full | rejection → chip |
  | T4 | `base64ToBytes(derivative.base64 ?? '')` capture.ts:57 → normalization.ts | **`typeof atob !== 'function'` — THE PRIME SUSPECT**: WO-4.2C's own verifier flagged "Hermes provides atob" as UNVERIFIABLE in-sandbox; the founder's device said otherwise | `ExifLeakError` → chip |
  | T5 | same site, empty input | `saveAsync` returned no base64 despite the option (would contradict the shipped contract — see the .d.ts quote below) | `ExifLeakError` → chip |
  | T6 | `atob(b64)` on invalid characters | `InvalidCharacterError` — a FOREIGN error class, not ours | uncaught class → chip |
  | T7 | `assertExifFree(bytes)` capture.ts:58 | genuine APP1/Exif in the output bytes | `ExifLeakError` → chip |
  | T8/T9 | `renderMetricsFrame` + its decode capture.ts:62-64 | same classes as T2–T6 | rejection → chip |
  | ∅ | `App.tsx takeShot catch` | ANY of the above | ONE undifferentiated chip — the incident: the founder's screenshot could not tell us which |
- **Part A item 3 — API shapes verified against the INSTALLED packages' shipped types (quoted): NO mismatch.** expo-image-manipulator 14: `manipulate(source: string | SharedRef<'image'>): ImageManipulatorContext` (ImageManipulator.types.d.ts:118) · `resize(size: { width?: number | null; height?: number | null }): ImageManipulatorContext` + `renderAsync(): Promise<ImageRef>` (ImageManipulatorContext.d.ts) · `saveAsync(options?: SaveOptions): Promise<ImageResult>` (ImageRef.d.ts:19) with `SaveOptions = { base64?: boolean; compress?: number; format?: SaveFormat }` (types:89-104) and `ImageResult.base64?: string` — "It is included if the `base64` save option was truthy" (types:18-24; we pass `base64: true`). expo-camera 17: `takePictureAsync(options?: CameraPictureOptions): Promise<CameraCapturedPicture>` (CameraView.d.ts:100), options `quality?: number; base64?: boolean; exif?: boolean; skipProcessing?: boolean` — our `{ quality: 1 }` is shape-correct; `CameraCapturedPicture {uri, width, height, base64?, exif?}` and we consume uri/width/height. **The one unverifiable-by-types dependency on the path was the `atob` global — exactly the assumption removed.**
- **Part A item 2 — the fix, `3bdead2`: `base64ToBytes` is now a PURE RFC 4648 decoder** (lookup table, ~30 lines, zero deps, no runtime global). Fail-closed RETAINED where it belongs: empty input · length%4==1 · any character outside the alphabet → `ExifLeakError(detail:'decode_failed')` — never a vacuous pass, never silent wrong bytes. T4 (the prime suspect) and T6 (the foreign error class) are now STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE; T5's contract violation would surface as decode_failed with its named code. EXIF fixtures proven BYTE-IDENTICAL through the new decoder against node's reference decoder — padded + unpadded + every %4 remainder (test/capture-incident.test.ts).
- **Part A item 4 — the diagnostic surface, preview-only:** `ExifLeakError` carries `detail`; `failureDetailOf(error)` maps deterministically (guard errors → their code · /permission|denied/-shaped rejections → `permission` · rest → `capture_failed`); the chip keeps « La photo n'a pas pu être prise — réessayez. » in EVERY profile, and preview builds add « détail : {code} » gated `{IS_PREVIEW && failureDetail !== null && …}` — the exact banner law (babel-inlined member expression, proven both ways by the banner test pattern: profile signal negative + source-gating pin). **If the founder's failure recurs post-fix, his screenshot now carries the answer.** (Named honestly: T1-class native rejections — e.g. tapping before the camera is ready — remain possible and will now read `détail : capture_failed`.)
- **Part B item 5 — categories 4→9** (mode · tissus · chaussures · sacs & accessoires · beauté scellée · maison · petit électroménager · enfants & bébé · artisanat), each with its own per-shot framing line in the register (neutral/instruction, lint-clean). **JOURNAL NOTE, verbatim per the order: "these are CAPTURE-GUIDANCE categories only — the launch/prohibited category list remains an open Decision; no policy is created here."** The module docblock names the Decision; the no-policy test proves no allow/deny machinery exists in code.
- **Part B item 6 — la caméra devient l'écran:** full-bleed width (`marginHorizontal: -theme.spacing.lg` — the SAME token the content pads with, proven paired by test), flex height to the tab bar (SafeAreaView owns the safe area); guidance banner overlaid TOP with the shot-intent line and the category recall chip inside it; guides scale (corners edge-anchored on `absoluteFillObject`); ONE primary action overlaid bottom-center in thumb reach (`width: '80%'`, min target from the kit). Zero-hardcode layout proven by test: the only bare numerics in the new styles are 0 (edge anchors) and 1 (flex). *Test 5 secondes : la caméra est l'écran — je cadre grand, j'appuie en bas, je vois exactement ce que verra l'acheteur.*

- **Founder device evidence, verbatim: « détail : exif_leak »** — the WO-4.2D diagnosis channel did its job on its first outing: the iOS encoder PRESERVES EXIF through `saveAsync`'s re-encode, and the fail-closed guard correctly refused the capture. DROPS the metadata carriers: **APP1 (Exif AND XMP), APP13 (IPTC/Photoshop), COM**. KEEPS everything structural or rendering-relevant: SOI · APP0/JFIF · APP2/ICC · APP14/Adobe · DQT · SOF* · DHT · DRI · standalone TEM/RSTn · the SOS→EOI entropy stream VERBATIM. Malformed streams REFUSE (`strip_failed` — the « détail » channel keeps working; five malformed classes tested). The capture path is now **decode → STRIP → `assertExifFree(stripped)` as a TRUE POST-CONDITION → the STRIPPED bytes ship as a data URI** (`bytesToBase64`, the decoder's proven inverse) — previewed AND stored: **WYSIWYG shows the shipped bytes literally.** The `saveAsync` file uri (which the founder's device proved can carry EXIF) never ships.

REGARDLESS: because the preview now renders the exact shipped bytes, any rotation or color defect the strip could ever introduce is **founder-visible by construction** on the very next capture.
- **Fixture suite (test/exif-strip.test.ts, 8 tests):** dirty multi-marker JPEG (JFIF+Exif+COM+DQT+SOF0+DHT+IPTC+SOS+entropy) stripped to the EXACT expected byte sequence · XMP-APP1 dropped · clean JPEG **byte-identical pass-through** (ICC kept) · the WO-4.2D verifier's own crafted layouts · TEM handling · five malformed classes → `strip_failed` · `bytesToBase64` equals the reference encoder (every %3 remainder) and round-trips through our decoder · **the full chain runs with `globalThis.atob` deleted**, ending in a `data:image/jpeg;base64,/9j…` URI whose payload decodes back to exactly the stripped bytes.
- **Test evolution, named:** the WYSIWYG pins evolved from « ships `derivative.uri` » to « ships `data:image/jpeg;base64,${bytesToBase64(stripped)}` and `uri: derivative.uri` appears nowhere » — strictly stronger. One tripwire self-catch: my strip error messages said "JPEG segment…" and MY OWN no-inference scan (/segment/) refused them — reworded to marker/block; the tripwire stays strict.
- **Evidence at `0bccd27` (my own runs, logs kept):** tests **67/67** · typecheck 0 · **ALL GATES GREEN** (EVIDENCE_DIR capture) · serial isolated dual export exit 0 ×2 — **Android 698 modules / 1.52 MB · iOS 700 / 1.52 MB; budget delta vs WO-4.2D: +0 modules** (pure JS only, as ordered) · **cold proof green and byte-exact** (fresh `--local` clone, frozen install 0, gates 0, android export 0, **md5 `046f4187287433914abbccbf358b2c7f` identical cold vs in-repo, same flags**). ## 2026-07-12 · WO-5.1 (the Grand Teint SUBSTRATE — design ref · typeface · icons · 2 deps · THE COLD-START PROOF) · in review — branch `e5/wo-5.1`
- **Why this slice exists:** de-risk the one thing that could make a beautiful design fail — Expo loads custom fonts ASYNCHRONOUSLY. No screens, no kit rebuild, no token consumption. Anchoring: branch off main `d7bc090` (WO-4.2E done; local==origin).
- **The founder's bundle carried NO fonts.** design-reference/grand-teint/ (55 files: prototypes · docs · tokens.json · 26 icons · share-cards · README) committed BYTE-EXACT (`diff -rq` clean vs the upload; **sha256 of every file in the packet, bundle-sha256.txt**). Archivo is item 2 — a separate acquisition (OFL 1.1) sourced from Google Fonts (`fonts.gstatic.com` latin variable, v25) and subset in-sandbox with **fontTools 4.63.0 + brotli** (provenance + per-file sha256 in `assets/fonts/BUILD.md`; generator in the packet — reproducible).
- **THE TYPEFACE — measured, and the founder-decision trigger does NOT fire:**
  - **Web variable woff2 (Latin subset, wght 400-900): 30,352 B compressed** — far under the design's own 65-80 KB estimate and ~14 % of the 300 KB gate once consumed (buyer-pwa, shop-plus).
  - **RN static set (400/500/700/800/900, Latin): 170,704 B = 166.7 KB per app** — inside the design's 180-240 KB estimate.
  - The typeface busts NEITHER budget on EITHER surface, so I did not have to bring the founder the typeface-vs-budget choice.
- **THE COLD-START PROOF (assets/fonts/COLD-START.md):** on RN the substrate is `src/ui/fonts.ts` — DATA (family `Archivo`, fallback `System`, five weights) that loads nothing and gates nothing (test-proven: no `expo-font`/`loadAsync`/`require`). The font is a progressive enhancement loaded BEHIND a fallback that paints first, so it CANNOT push cold-start past the D17 < 5 s budget. **Method limit, stated plainly:** no Android Go device/emulator exists in the sandbox — the device stopwatch is the founder's Expo Go pass (the established law). The sandbox proves the mechanism (never blocks first paint) + the byte cost; the genuine millisecond + CLS=0 measurement lives on the web (shop-plus buyer-pwa: Playwright under Go-class throttling proves `font-display: optional` never blocks first paint and **CLS = 0** across the swap, with a metrics-matched fallback). **Verdict: PASS — the typeface does not threaten the cold-start budget.**
- **THE ICON SET:** the 26 canonical glyphs → `src/ui/icons.tsx` react-native-svg components (currentColor via the `color` prop, default 20 dp), GENERATED from the design-reference SVGs (`scripts/gen-icons.py`, reproducible). Icon byte cost: icons.tsx **12,130 B**; the 26 source SVGs 7,356 B. Test proves geometry BYTE-IDENTITY (every path `d`/circle/rect carried verbatim) + currentColor threading + zero hardcoded color.
- **THE TWO APPROVED DEPS (founder rulings 2026-07-12):** `react-native-svg 15.12.1` + `expo-haptics ~15.0.8` — SDK-54 bundled, registry-derived from `expo/bundledNativeModules.json`. A test asserts exactly these two and nothing else entered.
- **⚠ one honest gap:** Archivo lacks **U+202F** (the narrow no-break space `Intl.NumberFormat('fr-FR')` emits between franc thousands). It renders invisible from the system fallback and does NOT affect tabular digit alignment (`tnum` fixes each DIGIT's width). Named for the design's decision at the next type slice (accept fallback · U+00A0 · U+2009).
- **Evidence at `f936818` (build `0a64480` + generator-pin follow-up) (my own runs, logs kept):** tests **75/75** (grand-teint 8 new) · typecheck 0 · **ALL GATES GREEN** (EVIDENCE_DIR capture) · serial isolated dual export exit 0 ×2 — **Android 698 / iOS 700 modules, byte-IDENTICAL hbc to WO-4.2E (`dc876b1f…`/`1121defe…`): +0 bundle modules because the substrate is UNWIRED** (no screen imports it — the running app is untouched; the fonts add to the APK asset payload separately, measured). **Cold proof green:** fresh `--local` clone, frozen install 0, ALL GATES GREEN.
- **Note — the namespace-fix round (a parallel build agent's find):** the shared generator's PWA branch emitted `<ns0:path>`; boutik has no PWA module so its RN `icons.tsx` was byte-identical before and after the fix (verified by regeneration) — the pinned generator `f936818` carries the fix for reproducibility parity only.

Two coupled blockers, both CONFINED to the PWA icon deliverable: **① every PWA inline icon rendered NOTHING** — the generator emitted `<ns0:path xmlns:ns0=…>` (getBBox measured 0×0 in a real browser); **② the geometry test was VACUOUS** (canon failure-mode #7: it grepped `d="…"` and stayed green while every icon was invisible). RN `icons.tsx` byte-identical (fix touches only the PWA branch). Build + payload + cold-start + rendering e2e all green. **This is the parallel-review model working: my own first web test let the defect through; the fresh-context reviewer (and a parallel builder) caught it.**

### WO-6.0 · migration-fact rulings (founder, 2026-07-12) + first tested unit
- **RULING ① (emoji → canonical icons + a GATE):** the 5 emoji in `App.tsx` (`📷✓🏠🏷️⏱️`) become react-native-svg components from canon's 26. Per ruling ① I will NOT substitute a lookalike; when I wire the TabBar I resolve the real derivation from the prototype's product nav, and if it is a genuine gap I STOP and name it (candidate: a canon icon-set bump). `colis`/`horloge`/`gains` exist but must not be guessed into place.
- **RULING ② (motion — token fidelity wins):** `motion.springSoft`/`springPop`/`flyOut` are cubic-bezier STRINGS; the kit drives `Animated.timing` + `Easing.bezier(...)`, never `Animated.spring({damping,stiffness,mass})` (that would invent design values). **Built + tested `src/ui/motion.ts` `cubicBezierPoints(curve)`** — a single shared PURE helper (no RN import, unit-testable): `test/motion-easing.test.ts` (4/4 green) asserts the extracted points EXACTLY equal the live canon tokens (`springSoft`→[0.2,0.8,0.25,1], `springPop`→[0.34,1.56,0.64,1], `flyOut`→[0.16,0.8,0.3,1]) AND that a keyword/malformed curve THROWS (no silent fallback = no drift). `useNativeDriver:true` everywhere (transform+opacity only). **If canon's motion group cannot express something the prototype genuinely needs (e.g. a true spring bounce) → CANON GAP: STOP, name, quote — never an app-local invention.**

### WO-6.0 · RULING ② — Archivo embedded NATIVELY, proven in the built artifact
- **Mechanism proven, not asserted (the founder's bar):** `app.json` declares the **`expo-font` config plugin** with the five static Archivo instances; `expo prebuild -p android` applied it and copied the fonts into the **native** project — `android/app/src/main/assets/fonts/` (5 TTFs). They compile into the APK → present **at first frame**, before the JS bundle runs. **No `useFonts`/`loadAsync`/`Font.load` anywhere** — the typeface is never loaded asynchronously, so no FOUT / splash-hold / blank-screen is possible. `assets/fonts/EMBEDDING.md` records the artifact listing (family + weight class per embedded file). (`android/` is a regenerable build artifact, gitignored — not committed.)
- **⚠ WO-5.1 DEFECT found + fixed here (flagged — a defect in already-merged, founder-PASSED work):** the WO-5.1 subset left all five weight instances with IDENTICAL name tables — family « Archivo SemiBold », PostScript `ArchivoSemiBold-Regular` — a COLLISION that would make native embedding unable to address the five weights (only one would register). The **glyph outlines were correct** (OS/2 weight classes 400/500/700/800/900, distinct file hashes); only the name tables were wrong (the instancer pinned the axis without `updateFontNames`). Fixed by rewriting each name table to a distinct weight-specific family (`Archivo-Regular/Medium/Bold/ExtraBold/Black`); the fix is guarded by `test/font-embedding.test.ts` (reads the TTFs, asserts 5 distinct families + correct weight classes). WO-5.1's byte-cost/cold-start PASS stands — the names were never exercised there (data-only, loaded nothing); the defect only surfaces when wiring.
- **The one authorized new dep (founder FORBIDDEN allowed it):** `expo-font ~14.0.12` (SDK-54 bundled). The WO-5.1 dep-allowlist test updated to `['expo-font','expo-haptics','react-native-svg']`.
- **Weight mapping:** `src/ui/fonts.ts` `fontFamilyForWeight(wght)` maps each design weight to its embedded family; the type scale's `row`/reconcileLine **wght 600** has no shipped instance → nearest (ties heavier → `Archivo-Bold`), so no text drops to the system face mid-screen.
- **Evidence:** typecheck 0 · **88/88 tests (13 files)** incl. `font-embedding.test.ts` (5: plugin lists all 5 · no async load · per-weight family map · distinct name tables + weight classes · byte budget). Do NOT merge.

### WO-6.0 · RULING ③ — the money separator PAINTS (U+00A0 fallback for U+202F)
- **⚠ FINDING + fallback (flagged — money-path):** canon `money.groupSeparator`/`currencySuffix` = **U+202F** (narrow no-break space), but **Archivo has no U+202F glyph** (verified across every available subset incl. the 230-glyph latin; nor U+2009 thin space) — rendering it would tofu a box next to a franc. The founder's ruling ③ pre-authorized a fallback; the safest renderable one is **U+00A0 (NBSP)** — present in Archivo, no-break (« 11 500 F » never wraps), nearest to U+202F. Implemented: `formatFcfa` now normalizes every narrow/no-break space to U+00A0 (was silently normalizing fr-FR's U+202F to a **breaking** U+0020 — a latent money-wrap defect); the catalog's currency suffix « {amount} F » space → U+00A0. **The canon token stays U+202F (the intent); this is the display-layer fallback for a typeface that lacks the glyph — not a money-waterfall change (the byte-stable quotes are numeric, untouched).**
- **PROVEN (not asserted):** `test/money-render.test.ts` — `formatFcfa(11500)` yields codepoints `[3131 U+00A0 3500]` (no U+202F, no breaking space); the full « 11 500 F » carries U+00A0 before the franc; and reading the EMBEDDED Archivo cmaps (fontTools) proves **U+00A0 is drawable (has_00A0=true), U+202F is NOT (has_202F=false — why the fallback exists)**, and every codepoint in « 11 500 F » is in the font (missing=[]). typecheck 0 · **91/91 tests (14 files)** · copy-lint OK (103 entries, 0 violations).
- **For the founder (your call to override if you want a different fallback):** the alternatives were (1) keep U+202F and rely on the device's SYSTEM fallback font (risks tofu on a low-end device with no U+202F in its fallback — the exact trust failure ruling ③ names), or (2) add a U+202F glyph to Archivo (no available Archivo source has one to copy). U+00A0 is the safest that PAINTS today. If you'd rather push U+202F into canon's own font subset at v0.9.2, say so and I'll carry it; the display swaps back with no screen change.
- Do NOT merge.

## WO-6.5 · TIER RED — the two pilot-gating safety debts (branch e6/wo-6.5 off main aa18c25)

### Part A · B1.3 — the hostile-image ALLOW-LIST (immune system)
- **The ruling (WO-4.2E NB①) applied:** `stripJpegMetadata` (src/studio/normalization.ts) moved from a DROP-LIST to an ALLOW-LIST. The old stripper copied every segment except APP1/APP13/COM, kept APP0/APP2/APP14, and at SOS copied the rest of the stream VERBATIM — so a hostile APPn (APP2/ICC, APP15…), a post-SOS/post-EOI payload (polyglot), or ICC-borne data survived. Now: copies ONLY **SOI · DQT · SOF · DHT · SOS+entropy · EOI**; EVERY other segment is dropped BY DEFAULT; the entropy stream is bounded to its REAL EOI via a stuffing-aware marker scan (nothing after EOI ships); SOF dimensions are checked against a ceiling (`MAX_JPEG_EDGE_PX = 8192`, decompression-bomb / overflow); fill-bytes (0xFF runs) are consumed; malformed/oversize → fail-closed (`strip_failed`), never a crash.
- **Deliberate stricture (flagged):** APP0/JFIF and APP2/ICC are now DROPPED (a baseline JPEG decodes without JFIF; the derivative is already sRGB from the encoder, so a dropped ICC profile is assumed sRGB) — a chosen trade of a colour-profile carrier for dropping every non-image segment by default. DRI/restart-interval is not on the list; the shipped encoder emits baseline-no-restart, so no product regression (a hostile DRI is simply dropped).
- **THE CORPUS (test/hostile-corpus.test.ts) — 13 fixtures, EACH a NAMED outcome (clean | fail-closed):** APP2/ICC+APP15 metadata · post-scan COM · post-EOI ZIP polyglot · post-EOI HTML polyglot · multi-APP1 · fill-bytes · a valid-looking APP1 embedded in the entropy stream · lying-length header · decompression bomb (65535²) · dimension overflow (0x8000) · zero dimension · corrupt-after-SOI · PNG. Nothing silently passes.

### Part B · B2.1 — the DURABLE offline queue (an offline action that dies at reboot is a lie)
- **New src/offline/queue.ts `DurableQueue`** — storage-INJECTED (QueueStore), ordered, IDEMPOTENT by `command_id` (the E1 pattern — `EventEnvelope.command_id`, Execution Contract §3: "every event carries command_id… same command_id = same command"). Persists every mutation; `open()` RESTORES persisted state (the reboot path; a corrupt/wrong-version blob refuses into empty, never crashes). `deliver()` replays pending IN ORDER; a success marks delivered (never re-sent); a **PoisonError → 'failed' with a named reason** (a designed terminal, never a silent drop); a transient error retries to `maxAttempts` then fails — **a poison can never block the queue forever**; ordering among the delivered is preserved, a failed entry set aside.
- **New src/offline/expoStore.ts** — the production backend on **Expo's document store** ("safe from being deleted by the system"). The dep `expo-file-system ~19.0.23` is the founder-gated durable-storage dep; **forcing evidence is QUOTED from the installed .d.ts** in the module header (`class File { write(string|Uint8Array): void; text(): Promise<string>; exists; create() }`, `class Paths { static get document(): Directory }`). Imported ONLY by the app; the pure queue + its tests never touch native.
- **SURVIVAL PROVEN BY EXECUTION (test/offline-queue.test.ts, 7):** a REAL on-disk store (Node fs, temp file) — a fresh `DurableQueue.open()` over the same path is a genuine COLD BOOT reading bytes another instance wrote; nothing shared in memory. Proven: actions queued offline survive an app-kill and are STILL PENDING (in order) on cold boot, then deliver · a partial-delivery kill leaves only the undelivered survivor, the delivered ones NOT re-sent · idempotent replay (dedupe on enqueue; deliver-twice + re-open-and-deliver sends ONCE) · a poison → named 'failed', entries behind it still deliver in order · a transient failure retries to maxAttempts then fails honestly · the honesty law: a pending entry is never 'delivered' across a reboot until an actual successful send.
- **Wired into the app (real, not dead code):** App B7 offline-confirm PERSISTS a `fulfillment.ready.v1` to the durable queue; the queued state surfaces the REAL persisted pending count (`shell.queue_durable`); reconnect (offline→off) flushes it. journey/money/kit UNTOUCHED (verified: `git diff main...HEAD` shows journey.ts, kit.tsx, store.ts unchanged).
- **Catalog 139→140** (+`shell.queue_durable`), copy-lint 140/0.

## 2026-07-13 · WO B0.2-DUP — the create-door idempotency (🟠 AMBER · done, IN REVIEW · DO NOT MERGE)
- **Anchor (quoted):** `Boutik-Plus-Building-Plan.md:36` B0.2 — *"unverified cannot publish; **duplicate idempotent**."* The verify half is NOT this slice (the founder's split); the duplicate half is standalone. This is the one moderation-at-scale item the GP-BOUTIK grounding found startable now (everything else is E4-gated).
- **RED protocol honoured:** the 3 fixtures were written and run FIRST — **all 3 failed** before the code (`_review/B02DUP/logs/red-proof.txt`: `outcome` undefined; the second create returned `ok:true` where a collision was expected). The implementation turned them GREEN (3/3).
- **The build** (`services/catalog-service/src/product.ts`, `create()` only): idempotent by `ctx.command_id` (the minted key the offline queue already persists per intent). A successful create records `command_id → {versionId, variantId, fingerprint}` with `fingerprint = JSON.stringify(draft)` (the same comparison `offline/queue.ts:111` uses). **duplicate** = same key + same fingerprint → `{ok:true, outcome:'duplicate'}`, the **SAME version+variant identity**, `events:[]` (no re-emit — one `catalog.product_submitted.v1`, ever); **collision** = same key + different fingerprint → `{ok:false, reason:'idempotency_collision'}` (refused + surfaced, the first version never overwritten); **created** = a new key. Mirrors `offline/queue.ts:108-118`'s duplicate|collision vocabulary at the service door.
- **Idempotency runs BEFORE eligibility (a deliberate ordering):** a replay of an already-successful create returns `duplicate` regardless of the current `canPublish`; a create that FAILED on `canPublish` records nothing, so it never falsely collides on a later legitimate retry.
- **FORBIDDEN honoured:** `moderation.ts` **untouched** (`git diff main -- …/moderation.ts` empty); `decide()`/`timeoutModeration()`/`activate()`/`revise()` bodies unchanged (the diff hunks are all inside `create()`/`CreateOutcome`/the intents map); no verify/eligibility rule added.
- **Evidence:** typecheck 0 (11/11; catalog now type-checks `test/`, so the new fixture is gate-typechecked) · catalog-service **16/16** (create-idempotency 3 + moderation 8 + product 3 + health 2) · full suite 19/19 · full **warm** `run-gates.sh` **ALL GREEN**. Fixed-clock: create takes an injected `at`; the idempotency store is per-instance, deterministic.
- **COLD-GATES PROOF (packet `logs/coldgates.log`, HEAD bfe9fcf):** fresh HOME (auth line = HTTPS→proxy `insteadOf`, NOT ssh) + fresh EMPTY store + `--frozen-lockfile` + fresh clone of the pushed branch → `run-gates.sh` **exit 0, ALL GATES GREEN from nothing** (cold HEAD bfe9fcf, frozen install exit 0, cold contracts 0.9.6, every negative fired).
- **FRESH-CONTEXT VERIFIER — VERDICT: PASS · BLOCKERS: 0** (on the final bytes bfe9fcf; full report `logs/verifier-report.md`). By its own hands: 5 scratch tests pass (duplicate → same identity + events:[] + no pv-2; collision → refused + first version untouched; distinct keys → distinct versions; **idempotency-before-eligibility both directions** — a canPublish-false create records nothing so a later same-key retry is 'created', and a successful create replayed with canPublish-false still returns 'duplicate'). **MUTATION-tested the guard:** neutering the collision line (→ `if (false && …)`) flipped BOTH the shipped ② and its scratch collision test; neutering the duplicate short-circuit flipped the shipped ①; both reverted (the tests bite). FORBIDDEN re-confirmed (`git diff main -- …/moderation.ts` empty; `scripts/gates/` empty; decide/timeout/activate/revise untouched). typecheck 0 (catalog test/ included), catalog 16, run-gates exit 0. Repo left clean.
- **VERIFIER OBSERVATION (recorded, not a defect):** the create fingerprint is `JSON.stringify(draft)` only (vs the offline queue's `name`+`JSON.stringify(payload)`) — correct here because the `command_id` already scopes the intent to a single "create" command; noted as a design fact.

## 2026-07-13 · WO Batch B · B1 — the settlement projection read model (🔴 RED · done, IN REVIEW · DO NOT MERGE)
- **RED protocol honoured: adversarial tests written and run FIRST, before the read model existed** (`_review/B1/logs/red-proof.txt`: the suite failed to even load — "Does the file exist?" — proving it bites). Then the implementation turned them GREEN (7/7).
- **The read model** (`apps/supplier-app/src/settlement/readModel.ts`) is a PURE reducer `projectReceivables(events) → Map<orderId, SettlementObligation>` over the authoritative `settlement.supplier_payable` + `payout.*` events, consuming the canon `SettlementObligationSchema` (never redefined). Boutik+ READS settlement (§5.2 owner: Ledger&Settlement); it holds no funds, keeps no running total, offers no way to pull money out.
- **⚠ B+I-05 — the displayed receivable EQUALS the locked obligation, NEVER recomputed** — proven adversarially: the reducer copies `amount` VERBATIM from the event (imports no waterfall, does no arithmetic on the amount); a test feeds `8_499` (a franc no round-input waterfall yields) and asserts it survives to the franc across Processing→Paid. B+I-05 held.
- **⚠ B7.1 — Paid ONLY after a provider-confirmed ref** — proven adversarially: `payout.submitted` → `Processing` (no ref, honest « en attente »); `payout.paid` WITHOUT a `payoutRef` → **NO transition** (a payout with no provider ref is not a payout — stays Processing); `payout.paid` WITH a ref → `Paid` + ref recorded. `Failed`/`Held` are honest terminals, not a generic collapse.
- **B10 « Mes recettes » wired to the real model** — the demo enum is gone (it filtered products by the fulfillment `status`); B10 now renders `world.receivables` (projected from seeded settlement EVENTS, not hand-set states): the **LOCKED amount** (`formatFcfa(obligation.amount)`, never recomputed) + its honest state chip/line (pre-Paid → « en attente », Processing → « versement en cours », Paid → « versé » + the provider ref shown, Held → « en révision », Failed → honest retry). Money register, copy-lint 166/0. The certified-mock seam (E4) is where the provider ref arrives — the UI never pretends a payout exists without it (the D5 doctrine, supplier-side).
- **Gate work — no-wallet-no-funds EXTENDED to the settlement surface** (`gates/fixtures/negative/no-wallet-no-funds/settlement-balance.ts`): a settlement wallet with a local balance + a withdrawal → the gate catches it (negative now 21 hits). The gate already bans `balance`/`withdraw`; the fixture proves settlement-surface coverage. A `recompute` GATE was deliberately NOT added — "recompute" appears only in comments forbidding it (the A1 precedent: the law's own text would false-positive); B+I-05 is proven by the adversarial TEST (the stronger, behavioural proof).
- **FLAG — my own B1 prose tripped two strict gates (the A1 false-positive class, again):** the words « no local balance / no withdrawal » in my read-model COMMENT and the B7.1 spec-quote hit the `no-wallet-no-funds` money gate; the `⚠` symbols in two `describe()` labels hit `no-emoji`. **On a MONEY gate I did NOT narrow the gate — I reworded my own prose** (kept every gate byte-unchanged) and paraphrased the B7.1 quote to avoid the guarded tokens. Recorded so the property is known: the funds-holding vocabulary cannot appear even in comments, by design.

## 2026-07-13 · WO Batch A · A1 — the real moderation state machine (🟠 AMBER · done, IN REVIEW · DO NOT MERGE)
- **The `approved_e1_sandbox` stub is DEAD.** `catalog-service/src/product.ts` no longer stamps a fake approval at create. A new listing is born `submitted` (emitting `catalog.product_submitted.v1`) and reaches `approved` ONLY by an Ops decision. The moderation state machine lives in the new `catalog-service/src/moderation.ts`.
- **Canon states, derived from the governing sentence** (ECOSYSTEM-MASTER-REFERENCE Part 9 / B+3: *"draft → submitted → changes-requested (with specific reasons) → approved → paused → retired"*; B2.2: *"Moderation timeout = pending"*). Built: `submitted → changes_requested(+reasons) | approved`, and `timeout → pending`. **FLAG ①:** the founder's WO shorthand said "approved | rejected" — canon's term is **`changes_requested`** (a correctable rejection with specific reasons); I built the CANON vocabulary. `draft/paused/retired` are later-lifecycle — NOT invented here (derive-or-stop).
- **B+I-01 enforced at activation, structurally:** `activate()` now refuses `not_approved` for any version whose `moderationState !== 'approved'` (submitted/pending/changes_requested all fail closed). A revision (`revise`) RE-ENTERS moderation (`submitted`) — a change can never inherit the prior approval (B+I-01/B+I-04).
- **The 4 canon events emitted with canon envelopes** (`PlatformEventSchema`, consumed never redefined): `catalog.product_submitted.v1` (on submit) · `media.derivative_approved.v1` (on approve) · `catalog.blocked.v1` + `media.asset_rejected.v1` (on changes_requested — Desk 3 covers *"facts, media, and categories"* in one decision, so one decision emits both the catalog and media events, BOTH carrying the specific reasons).
- **No self-moderation (Desk 3: *"a supplier can never approve his own listing"*)** — TWO proofs: (runtime) `decide()` refuses any non-operator actor via actor-provenance (`ops:moderation:` prefix — the sera custody-spine pattern), leaving the state untouched; (structural absence) `apps/supplier-app/test/no-self-moderation.test.ts` scans the whole app source and asserts there is NO decide/approve/setModerationState lever — the sera D1/D3 "nothing for a supplier-side path to call" pattern. The Ops decision arrives via a contract-honouring mock actor for E4; the live Desk-3 console is PLATFORM's half, not built here.
- **A silent rejection is UNREPRESENTABLE** (sera bare-`failed` precedent): `ModerationDecisionSchema` is a strict discriminated union whose `changes_requested` variant REQUIRES `reasons: z.array(...).min(1)` — a reasonless rejection fails strict-parse AND is a compile error (a `@ts-expect-error` pins the type-level refusal).
- **FLAG ②:** the reason CODE SET (`facts_incomplete · no_public_safe_proof · price_or_contact_in_image · not_neutral_packaging · prohibited_or_unlaunched_category · authenticity_concern`) is **derived, each code traced to an enumerated requirement** (B+I-01 · B+I-02/Desk 3 · B+3 · M2/M4 category decision · §2), because the spec names the RULE (*"specific, actionable reasons"*) but does not enumerate codes. ⏳ Flagged for founder ratification of the exact set — not invented free-hand.
- **B11 « Modération » wired to the REAL state** — the demo enum abuse is gone (it keyed moderation copy off the FULFILLMENT `status`, a category error: `echeance_depassee` is not a moderation state). Demo products carry a real `moderationState` (canon-vocab UI mirror; the service is authoritative) INDEPENDENT of the fulfillment `status`; B11 renders approved/submitted/pending/changes_requested honestly, lists the specific reasons on changes_requested, and shows an honest **offline note** (last-known state, nothing confirmed until the network returns — never a fake approval). French Voice register neutral; copy-lint 153/0.
- **FLAG ③ — A1 surfaced a false positive in A2's just-merged gate and I NARROWED it (touched a merged slice — your veto):** A1's legitimate reason codes `price_or_contact_in_image` + `not_neutral_packaging` on one line contain "contact" + "packaging", which A2's co-occurrence gate flagged as a violation. The English word **"packaging" only ever appears in code identifiers** (the app's real packaging copy is French: colis/emballage/paquet), so it earned only false positives — I dropped it from the gate's exterior-token set and fixed the one fixture line that relied on it. The gate's real coverage (French copy) is UNCHANGED; positive clean, negative still fires 15 hits. Flagged because it edits the A2 gate you merged at `e42888f`.
- **FLAG ④:** `zod` is now a declared dep of `catalog-service` (it imports `z` for the decision schema). offer-service imports zod too but relies on accidental hoisting (a latent phantom dep) — I did NOT fix offer-service (out of scope); I declared it properly where A1 needs it. **FLAG ⑤:** the 2 offer-service test fixtures carrying `moderationState:'approved_e1_sandbox'` moved to `'approved'` (the honest value for their `status:'active'` products); no stub string survives anywhere.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · BOUTIK-MEDIA-1 — the photo pillar: real image bytes, opaque keys, on the wire
- **THE HARD GATE — media moderation is deliberately absent (founder ruling 2026-07-24; record this so nobody rebuilds it):** *a product image inherits `ProductVersion.moderationState`, and `buildSupplyProjection` (`projection.ts:40`) is the only gate — an unapproved product never projects its refs. When a second supplier onboards, image review becomes necessary; the retrofit point is `ProductVersion.moderationState` (canon, already wired) plus a live/moderation check on the media read route (the shop-plus `src/index.ts:90-99` hole). This is a policy change, not an architecture one — do NOT build a parallel media-moderation state machine that duplicates the product gate.*
- **Grounding first (BOUTIK-MEDIA-GROUND, read-only, accepted):** media-service was a `makeHealthFetch` stub + a PURE `premium-frame.ts` with **zero concept of bytes**; two header-vs-body discrepancies named (the "authoring surface serving views" that serves none; "EXIF is STRIPPED"/"master stays PRIVATE" where nothing is stripped and `private/…` is a string, not stored private storage).
- **Key shape (founder ruling — the highest-stakes item):** `media/{crypto.randomUUID()}`, product link SERVER-SIDE in the offer entry, never in the URL. Servable/unguessable/identity-free sit on INDEPENDENT axes; the wrong instinct — the one shop-plus fell into (`storefronts/{id}/…`, and earlier a sequential `media-N`) — is deriving unguessability from an identity, which here would ALSO make offer-service's own `assertAssetRefsIdentityFree` reject every ref this service produced. `mintMediaKey()` takes **no arguments by design** — no parameter through which a caller could smuggle a pv, a supplier id, or a counter into the key. THE TOKEN IS THE ONLY WALL (no review step, no read-route gate) — 122 bits CSPRNG, `productVersionId` contributes ZERO entropy (it is on the wire).
- **Ported PLUMBING, not POLICY:** the `MediaStore` port + env-gated `resolveMediaStore` (R2 iff `env.BUCKET`, else in-memory — CI can never reach real storage, by construction); the pure-JS magic-byte sniff + PNG-IHDR/JPEG-SOF dimension read + size/dim bounds; the private-bucket read route with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`. NOT ported: the `pending_review`/`approve`/`reject` machine (shop-plus's `approve()` has NO production caller — held media invisible with no path to live; that trap is not reproduced), the audio path, and the identity-namespaced key.
- **Revocation built now (what makes the deferred read-route gate survivable):** `MediaStore.remove`; `revoke(key)` deletes the object; `replace(old, bytes)` mints a NEW token then deletes the old — new-before-delete, so a crash leaves an orphan (costs storage) not a hole (breaks a live image), and a REFUSED replacement leaves the old image intact.
- **MEDIA KINDS — a consequence of the opaque-key ruling, stated plainly:** boutik's taxonomy (hero/proof/detail) is carried by the STRUCTURE of canon `ProductAssets`, NOT by the object key and NOT by a field on the service — an opaque key cannot describe a role. Every product image validates identically. media-service holds **no durable index**: the key→product link IS the offer entry's `ProductAssets`.
- **Wire mapping (founder rulings):** ALL images travel EXCEPT `masterRef` (private by construction, `premium-frame.ts:45`) — excluded from the wire AND from the cap count. **INDEX 0 IS THE HERO**, mandated in code + test, order `heroSquare · heroVertical · proof · detail…` (a flat `string[]` loses role, so order IS the signal). **No dedup, no reorder** — silently collapsing a supplier's declared assets modifies their data, the same objection as truncation.
- **CAP = 6 wire refs, and the founder's tighten-question answered from the bytes:** `heroSquare`/`heroVertical` are **crops of ONE photograph**, not distinct captures — `premium-frame.ts:39-48` derives both (and `proof`) from a single `input.captureRef` with a single `input.sha256`; `CaptureInput` is one capture. So 3–4 photographs legitimately yield 5–6 refs and a cap of 4 would reject a real product: **six confirmed, no tightening**. Enforced at the CREATE COMMAND with a typed refusal `too_many_asset_refs {max, presented}` in the existing ladder shape — **never truncated**, and an over-cap create persists nothing.
- **Carriage:** `assets?: ProductAssets` on the BOUTIK-LOCAL `CreateOfferCommand` + `OfferEntry` (no canon change — canon is not moved for a linkage only this producer needs; NOT smuggled into `facts`, which is free-form and would make the link unfindable). Optional: an offer with no images still emits `[]`, the honest empty, never a seed or demo image.
- **PROVEN BY TESTS:** media-service 33 (opaque-key shape/uniqueness/no-identity/arity-0, the boundary tooth refusing traversal + identity-keyed + sequential keys, magic-byte + real-header validation with typed refusals, exact-bytes round-trip, revoke/replace/refused-replace, resolver gating) · offer-service 64 (+12 new: masterRef excluded from wire AND cap, hero-at-0 through the REAL builder, no-dedup, cap 6 accept / 7 typed-refuse / nothing persisted, populated array parses @2.0.0 and the value-side guard still bites on a supplier-keyed ref) · typecheck 11/11 · build 9/9 · **ALL GATES GREEN** (run-gates.sh exit 0).
- **UNPROVEN UNTIL A REAL DEPLOY + A REAL UPLOAD (green CI proves less on infrastructure):** the R2 binding and bucket name in `wrangler.toml`; that a real JPEG/PNG from a real phone passes the sniff/dimension bounds; that `caches.default` actually populates and serves at a Cloudflare colo (the cache BRANCH is proven with an injected fake — the origin-sparing assertion is real, the *platform* behaviour is not); real R2 read latency/streaming; and the `MEDIA_PUBLIC_BASE` origin being correct. No deploy run — the founder runs deploys.
- **GAPS REPORTED, NOT FILLED:** (1) **6 package.json files still declare contracts at `d7b27bb`** while the pnpm-workspace override forces `642f849` — commerce-core:22, media-service:18, catalog-service:18, inventory-service:25, supplier-service:18, fulfillment-service:23. Everything INSTALLS 2.0.0 (override wins; lockfile records 642f849), so it is a declaration disagreement, not a functional break — but it violates the bump law (override + lockfile + package.json must agree). Introduced by SUPPLY-DISPLAY-PRODUCER-1, which bumped only root + offer-service. Founder to rule on the sweep. (2) `premium-frame.ts` derives `proof` from the SAME capture+sha256 as the hero, so today's builder cannot express an independent actual-item proof photograph, and `detail` is always `[]` — the pure builder cannot express multiple photographs at all. It is not wired to media-service. (3) No upload route is exposed on the Worker (supplier-app write path out of scope) — when it lands it MUST carry the shared-secret write gate.
- **OUT OF SCOPE (untouched):** supplier-app write path, catalog-service, supply wire transport + auth, deploy.
- **DO NOT MERGE** — CI green on-branch; awaiting founder approval.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · BOUTIK-MEDIA-1 — verifier finding: REVOCATION DOES NOT REVOKE (open, founder to rule)
- **The defect (fresh-context verifier, then reproduced independently by me against the same code):** `revoke()` deletes the R2 object, but the read route serves `caches.default` BEFORE the bucket and stamps `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`. There is no purge anywhere. Probe output: read #1 → `200 SUPPLIER-PHOTO`; R2 object present after revoke → `false`; read #2 AFTER revoke → **`200 SUPPLIER-PHOTO`**, total origin reads `1` (the second read never touched the bucket). A browser that fetched once holds the bytes for a year with no revalidation path.
- **Why it is load-bearing, not cosmetic:** founder ruling 7 DEFERRED the read-route moderation gate explicitly *on the strength of revocability* ("Revocation does not wait — it is what makes the deferral survivable, because without it a leaked or stale ref stays live forever with nothing able to revoke it"). As built, a leaked or stale ref DOES keep resolving from cache. The deferral's justification does not currently hold.
- **My error, named:** I wrote three comments asserting the image "stops resolving" after revoke — behaviour the code does not deliver. That is the header-vs-body class I flagged in others' code during grounding. All three corrected to state the true scope (`remove` destroys the ORIGIN copy only).
- **NOT FIXED — the founder rules the caching policy** (it trades cost against takedown latency, and edge caching was put in scope for cost). Options: (a) purge on revoke — note `cache.delete` is COLO-LOCAL, so global purge needs the CF API/cache-tags, i.e. partial by itself; (b) split the layers — short browser `max-age` + long edge `s-maxage`, and drop `immutable` (which explicitly forbids revalidation), so revocation propagates in minutes while the edge still absorbs fan-out — **my recommendation**; (c) accept it as a stated, bounded risk. Any of (a)/(b) needs a regression test wiring revoke → read.
- **Other verifier findings, all corrected in-place (comment honesty, no behaviour change):** `media-key.ts` claimed `Math.random` is "banned repo-wide by the mint-path gate" — FALSE, that gate (`check-mint-path-entropy.mjs:31`) only scans `command-id*`/`commandId*` basenames, so the slice's highest-stakes file is unscanned (widening the glob = named follow-up); `projection.ts` and `supply-endpoint.ts` carried stale "assetRefs is [] today" comments; `media-service/src/index.ts` still described itself as a WO-B0.1 stub.
- **Verifier PASSED everything else** with file:line evidence: opaque key shape + arity-0 mint + gate-before-lookup, resolver gating, magic-byte validation, immutable header + edge-cache origin-sparing, masterRef exclusion, hero-at-0 through the real builder, cap-6 typed refusal with zero truncation paths (grepped `.slice(`/`.splice(`/`.length =`) and nothing persisted on refusal, no policy ported, no canon touched, and all suites green.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · BOUTIK-MEDIA-1 — revocation FIXED: bounded-latency takedown (founder ruling, Option B + bounded edge TTL)
- **THE ROOT ERROR, named so it does not recur (founder's words, adopted):** *content-immutability was conflated with authorization-permanence.* A new upload mints a new key, so the bytes at a key never change — which made a year-long cache look safe. But revocation is not the content changing, it is **access being withdrawn**. Different property; the header was set for the wrong one.
- **THE PROPERTY, stated as it actually is — write it this way, never as plain "revocable":** **BOUNDED-LATENCY REVOCATION, NOT INSTANT TAKEDOWN.** `revoke()` destroys the origin object immediately and best-effort purges the serving colo; other colos and already-served browsers keep answering from cache until TTL. **A leaked ref keeps resolving for UP TO the edge TTL (1 h).** Previously that window was a year of `immutable` with nothing able to close it. **The second-supplier retrofit of the read-route moderation gate must start from this property, not from an assumption of instant takedown** — the deferral was authorised on the strength of revocability, and this is what revocability actually means here.
- **Built:** `immutable` DROPPED entirely (it explicitly forbids revalidation — the reason a browser held bytes for a year with no recourse). `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, s-maxage=3600` (`worker/index.ts` `CACHE_CONTROL`). **Both layers bounded** — the founder's correction to my framing: a short browser TTL alone fixes browsers and leaves the edge broken, because `cache.delete` is COLO-LOCAL, so a revoke purges the serving colo while every other colo keeps serving for the full `s-maxage`. The TTL, not the purge, is what bounds worst-case GLOBAL takedown. Colo-local purge kept as best-effort (`makeEdgeCachePurge`) because it makes revoke-then-verify immediate in the common case.
- **Numbers sanity-checked as instructed, both sane, neither raised:** 300 s browser comfortably covers a buyer scrolling/re-rendering a vitrine (re-reads happen in seconds); 3600 s edge absorbs the burst that matters (a shared link opened many times in a short window) while capping worst-case global takedown at an hour. Correct call for a pre-buyer product: a year-long edge TTL optimises fan-out that does not exist while creating a hole nothing can close. Revisit only when cost actually bites.
- **REGRESSION TEST — mandatory, and the defect existed because it did not:** `test/revoke-read.regression.test.ts` (7 tests): read 200 → revoke → **read 404** (the exact sequence that used to return 200 twice); replace stops the OLD ref while the new one serves; the header is pinned (`no immutable`, `max-age=300`, `s-maxage=3600`, and an explicit assertion that `31536000` never returns); and — honestly — a colo the purge never reached **still serves 200**, pinning the residual window as a stated property rather than hiding it.
- **OPTION A CHECKED, founder's belief CONFIRMED (and worse than he thought):** Cloudflare's purge-by-URL is the ZONE endpoint (`POST /zones/{zone_id}/purge_cache`) and needs a zone in the account; `workers.dev` is Cloudflare's own domain, not a zone the founder owns, so purge-by-URL is unavailable on the current setup. Cache-tag purge is Enterprise-only. **Additional finding, higher-stakes:** Cloudflare documents the **Cache API as a no-op on `workers.dev` subdomains** — `caches.default` put/match do not cache there. If that holds on his deploy, then TODAY on `*.workers.dev` there is **no edge cache at all**: every read is a Worker invocation + an R2 GET (the fan-out protection the edge cache was put in scope for does not yet exist), and correspondingly no edge revocation window either (the browser window is real regardless). Getting real edge caching requires a custom domain on a zone — which would ALSO make Option A possible. **I cannot verify this from the sandbox (no Cloudflare network access); it is documented behaviour I am confident of, to be confirmed on the founder's first real deploy.** Either way the bounded TTL is the correct fix, since it holds in both worlds.
- **MINT-PATH GATE WIDENED (folded in, founder ruling):** `check-mint-path-entropy.mjs` scanned only `command-id*`/`commandId*`, leaving `media-key.ts` — the slice's highest-stakes file — unscanned by the gate that would catch a `Math.random` key. Glob widened to `media-key*`/`mediaKey*`; the Math.random check and CSPRNG non-vacuity token are UNCHANGED (widening scope is not weakening a gate). Proven both ways: gate now reports **2 mint paths** (was 1), and a **planted `Math.random` in media-key.ts FAILS it with exit 1** naming the file; the existing commandId negative fixture still fails as required.
- **Named honestly:** `makeEdgeCachePurge` is NOT wired to a deployed route — this Worker exposes no revoke/upload route (write path out of scope), so nothing calls it in production today. It is exercised by the service in tests; the write-path slice must pass it in when it adds the route. The bounded TTLs are what make revocation real in the meantime.
- **Green after the change:** typecheck 11/11 · media-service **40** tests (6 files, +7 regression) · offer-service 64 · ALL GATES GREEN.
- **DEFERRED TO THEIR OWN SLICES (founder-accepted, not this diff):** (1) the six package.jsons declaring contracts at `d7b27bb` while the override forces `642f849` — declaration-only slice after this merges, kept out so it does not muddy a security diff; (2) `premium-frame.ts` deriving `proof` from the same capture+sha256 as the hero with `detail` always `[]` — **the named blocker on a product ever carrying independent photographs**, and the supplier-authoring slice's work.
- **MERGED** at release **`7e2c178`** (anchor ec66163; merged tree byte-identical to the approved commit; frozen install + build + ALL GATES GREEN re-proved on merged main).

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · REPORT — Workers Cache (new product) + DEPLOY-TIME VERSION DRIFT (named class). Investigation only; nothing built.
**A. WHICH CACHE WORLD WE ARE IN TODAY — the founder must read no cost or takedown number as measured until this is settled.**
- Our deployed Workers run on `*.workers.dev`, and our `wrangler.toml` files carry `compatibility_date = "2025-07-05"` with **no `cache` block**. The edge-cache code in `media-service/worker/index.ts` uses the CLASSIC Cache API (`caches.default`), which Cloudflare documents as requiring a custom domain or route — a workers.dev deployment alone is not sufficient. **So on the real Worker today the edge branch does nothing:** no fan-out protection (every read = one Worker invocation + one R2 GET) and no edge revocation window either. The BROWSER window (`max-age=300`) is real regardless. The injected-fake test proves our branch logic, never the platform behaviour — as already stated in the UNPROVEN list.
**B. THE NEW WORKERS CACHE — founder's summary CONFIRMED against current docs, with one decisive correction.**
- **Availability:** "available today to every Worker on any plan" — free plan included, **no separate pricing** (requests bill at the standard Workers rate whether served from cache or not).
- **Zoneless / workers.dev:** yes — a Worker is a zoneless entity and "the cache follows the Worker wherever it runs — custom domain, workers.dev, service binding, preview, Workers-for-Platforms tenant". So edge caching on workers.dev is **not dead**; the fan-out protection is recoverable.
- **Opt-in:** explicit. `"cache": { "enabled": true }` in the Wrangler config, **Wrangler ≥ 4.107.0** (the founder's CLI is 4.114.0 — already sufficient). Docs examples pair it with a 2026-07-2x `compatibility_date`; ours is 2025-07-05, so **whether a newer compat date is required is UNCONFIRMED** (see D).
- **Purge:** by **tag** (`Cache-Tag` response header, consumed and stripped by Cloudflare), by **path prefix**, and `purgeEverything` — programmatically via `ctx.cache.purge()` or `import { cache } from 'cloudflare:workers'`. Same rate-limiting system as the zone purge API. **So Option A becomes viable WITHOUT a custom domain** — the exact constraint we both ruled it out on.
- **THE CORRECTION THAT MATTERS — they are two independent systems, not one:** the docs state the Cache API is "a separate programmatic cache store … independent of Workers Caching — operations on one do not affect the other, and `ctx.cache.purge()` is what invalidates Workers-Caching entries." **Consequence: enabling Workers Cache would NOT make our existing `caches.default` code work, and `ctx.cache.purge()` would NOT purge entries our code wrote.** Adopting it means REPLACING our hand-rolled edge layer (declarative, driven by the `Cache-Control` header we already emit) — not configuring it. Do not read "enable the flag and our cache starts working" into this.
- **What it would buy:** real edge fan-out protection on workers.dev, plus **near-instant revocation** via purge-by-prefix/tag instead of hour-bounded. That would let the second-supplier retrofit start from a stronger property than the one journaled above. Our `Cache-Control` (`max-age=300, s-maxage=3600`) already drives it correctly, so the bounded TTL stays the floor and purge becomes the fast path — the TTL is MORE load-bearing under a working edge cache, not less.
- **Other limits noted:** only GET/HEAD cached; no build-time pre-population (a response caches only after being served once); `cache.put` incompatible with tiered caching.
**C. NOT BUILT.** No caching rewrite, no config change, no deploy. Adopting Workers Cache is its own slice and needs a founder ruling; this entry is the investigation he ordered.
**D. UNVERIFIED, and I could not close it from here:** `developers.cloudflare.com` and `blog.cloudflare.com` are BLOCKED by this sandbox's egress proxy (WebFetch 403, curl `CONNECT tunnel failed, response 403`). Everything above comes from search-result extracts of those pages, not from reading them end to end. Specifically unconfirmed: whether a newer `compatibility_date` is required, the exact `cache.purge({...})` argument shape, and the precise purge rate limits. **Read the doc pages before writing any code against this.**

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · DEPLOY-TIME VERSION DRIFT — a named class (founder finding from the live deployment)
- **THE FINDING (founder, read from the Cloudflare API — not from the repo):** the deployed `offer-service` bundle contains **zero** occurrences of `productName`, `assetRefs`, `assertAssetRefsIdentityFree`. Created and last-modified within two seconds of each other, so it has been deployed exactly once and never redeployed — it **predates** the canon 2.0.0 producer migration merged at `39a8fea`. The write gate IS live (`OFFER_WRITE_SECRET` + `X-Write-Key` present), so the secret is set and the gate runs. **The live Worker still emits the five-field SupplyProjection while canon, this repo and shop-plus's consumer are all at 2.0.0** — a real pull would fail the strict parse. Harmless today (no supply wire exists, nothing pulls), real nonetheless.
- **THE GAP, owned:** after the producer migration merged I wrote that it "merged the code and deployed nothing" and that "the lockstep constraint moves to deploy time" — and then nobody checked. Predicting a failure mode is not the same as instrumenting it.
- **THE CLASS:** the workspace-override bump law enforces lockstep **across repos at MERGE time**. **Nothing in this project covers drift between a merged repo and its DEPLOYED ARTIFACT**, and the two are now independently versioned on both boutik and shop-plus. Merge-time green says nothing about what is running.
- **PROPOSAL (proposed, NOT built — founder ruling required): a deploy-freshness stamp on the health route.** Precedent: shop-plus already does this shape for the same reason — the real `expo-updates` `updateId` in the demo footer, « honest provenance for the device pass », after a stale Expo bundle wasted a session.
  - **Shape:** `GET /health` gains `release` (the git commit sha the bundle was built from) and `canon` (the pinned `@platform/contracts` version, e.g. `2.0.0`). Cheap version = sha only; the canon version is what actually catches THIS defect, because the sha alone would not have told anyone the wire shape was stale.
  - **How it gets there:** the deploy workflow injects them at bundle time (esbuild `--define`, from `github.sha` and the installed contracts `package.json` version). No runtime cost, no new dependency, no new route.
  - **What it makes possible:** a one-command answer to "is what is running the release I think it is" — `curl …/health` and compare — and, later, an optional CI check that fails a deploy-verification step when the deployed `release` ≠ the release being shipped.
  - **Honest limits:** it is a STAMP, not a guarantee — it reports what the bundle was built from, so it detects staleness and version skew but cannot detect a bundle whose contents were altered after build. It also cannot fire on its own: someone (or a scheduled check) must read it. That is still strictly better than the current state, where the only way to know was to read the deployed bundle out of the Cloudflare API by hand.
  - **Scope note:** boutik AND shop-plus both need it for the class to be closed; proposing the boutik shape here, the shop-plus lane would mirror it.
- **NOT REDEPLOYING (founder instruction):** the next deploy carries the 2.0.0 producer AND the BOUTIK-MEDIA-1 asset mapping together — one deploy, no intermediate state. The founder runs it.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · PROPOSAL — WORKERS-CACHE-ADOPT-1 (scoped and PARKED; not urgent, not a blocker, nothing built)
**Founder read the docs I was proxy-blocked from and confirmed my four answers, added two capabilities, and found a hazard neither of us saw. This is the scoped proposal he asked for. NOTHING HERE IS BUILT.**
- **THE HAZARD (founder, from the docs):** Cloudflare checks the cache **BEFORE running the Worker**; on a hit it returns the cached response **without executing any code**. The cache key is path + entrypoint + `ctx.props` + Worker version — **NOT request headers**. Automatic bypass fires only on a `Set-Cookie` response header or an `Authorization` request header. **Our write gate uses `X-Write-Key`, not `Authorization`, so it does NOT trigger the bypass.** Responses with no `Cache-Control` fall to **heuristic freshness** rather than being left uncached.
- **I AUDITED OUR SURFACES AGAINST THAT, AND IT IS WORSE THAN HYPOTHETICAL. Exactly ONE response in all of `services/` emits a `Cache-Control` header** — the media read route (`media-service/worker/index.ts:123`). **Every other response emits none:** the key-gated admin list `GET /offers`, `GET /supply-projection/:pv`, `/health`, and every 401/404. Under a naive `cache.enabled = true` on offer-service, the **key-gated admin list would be cached on one authorised request and then served to callers holding no key at all, with the auth check never running** — and no test we have would catch it, because our tests exercise the Worker directly while the cache sits in front of the Worker.
- **THE ARCHITECTURE SPLIT DECIDES THE SCOPE, and it is clean:**
  - **`offer-service` is ENTIRELY Durable-Object-backed** (`wrangler.toml:18` `OfferDO`; every route — POST /offers, GET /offers, GET /supply-projection — resolves through the DO). Workers Caching never caches DOs directly; caching a DO-backed read needs a named-entrypoint wrapper. Its routes are also either gated or freshness-critical. **RECOMMENDATION: adopt NOTHING on offer-service. Leave `cache.enabled` off for the whole service.**
  - **`media-service` is R2-backed with NO Durable Object** (`wrangler.toml:18` `[[r2_buckets]]` only). It is the ONE service with no DO-wrapping problem, the ONE already emitting a correct `Cache-Control`, and the only one whose route is genuinely fan-out-shaped. **RECOMMENDATION: adopt on `media-service` ONLY, and only on the media read route.**
- **WHAT IT BUYS ON THAT ROUTE:** tiered caching by default (first request anywhere on Earth populates the upper tier; later requests from any data centre skip the Worker) and **request collapsing** (many simultaneous requests for the same key run the Worker once) — which is precisely the shared-link burst we sized the TTL around, solved by the platform instead of by our TTL. Plus **purge by path prefix / tag**, which turns revocation from hour-bounded into near-instant. The cache key including **Worker version** is a bonus: a redeploy invalidates naturally.
- **WHAT REPLACES THE HAND-ROLLED LAYER (the crux — this is a REPLACEMENT, not a configuration):** the classic Cache API and Workers Caching are documented as INDEPENDENT stores. So `caches.default` + `makeEdgeCachePurge` in `media-service/worker/index.ts` would be **deleted**, not kept — leaving both would be two caches, one of them dead on workers.dev and misleading. `revoke()`/`replace()`'s purge hook rewires from `cache.delete(Request)` to `ctx.cache.purge({...})` (or the `cloudflare:workers` `cache` import where `ctx` is out of scope). Our existing `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, s-maxage=3600` already drives Workers Caching correctly and stays as-is — **the bounded TTL we merged is correct either way, and becomes the FLOOR under a purge fast-path rather than the only mechanism.**
- **NON-NEGOTIABLES, FIRST AND NOT AS LATER HARDENING (founder ruling):**
  1. **Every gated route returns `Cache-Control: private` EXPLICITLY, never by omission** — omission means heuristic freshness, which is how the hazard fires.
  2. **Per-entrypoint cache configuration** (the docs support an `exports` block with cache enabled per entrypoint) so caching is ON only for the media read route and OFF everywhere else — default-off, opt-in per route.
  3. **A test that asserts a gated route is never cacheable, structured to FAIL when someone later adds a route without the header** — i.e. enumerate the Worker's routes and assert each is either explicitly cacheable-by-design or explicitly `private`/`no-store`; a new unlisted route fails the test. This is the shape that would have caught the defect, rather than a per-route assertion that a new route silently escapes.
  4. **Explicit `no-store` on the media route's 404s.** They currently carry no header. Negative caching of a 404 is low-risk here (a fresh token per upload means a 404'd key never later becomes valid) but leaving it to heuristics is the same omission class as #1.
- **`GET /supply-projection` — I AGREE IT SHOULD NOT BE CACHED, with a sharper reason than "an invisible second freshness policy".** The payload carries its own `asOf`, so a cached response does NOT hide staleness — the true `asOf` travels with it and the consumer's bound still computes correctly. The real objection is stronger: **SW-2 BLOCKS agreement on staleness**, so caching would hand back stale-but-honest truth when fresh truth existed, and the consumer would then correctly refuse to act — **caching would manufacture the exact blocked state the wire exists to avoid.** Add that `available` (stock) is the most volatile field on it, and hour-old stock shown to a reseller deciding whether to list is a product harm, not a technicality. **Do not cache it.**
- **COMPATIBILITY DATE:** the docs state no minimum; their example sets it to the current date with a comment recommending that. Ours is `2025-07-05` on both services. **Treat as UNTESTED, not as a known requirement — settle it by trying it, not by assuming either way.** Wrangler ≥ 4.107.0 is required and the founder's CLI is 4.114.0, so that half is already satisfied.
- **SEQUENCING / PRIORITY:** **PARKED.** Boutik is the priority and this is optimisation, not a blocker — nothing pulls from these services yet, and today there is no edge cache at all (workers.dev + classic Cache API + no `cache` block). The bounded TTL holds in both worlds. **Do not start this before the supplier-authoring (write-path) slice**, which is what actually moves the founder's north star.
- **GAPS, NOT FILLED:** the exact `ctx.cache.purge({...})` argument shape, the purge rate limits, and the `exports`-block per-entrypoint syntax are still unread by me (proxy-blocked). **Whoever builds this reads those pages first** — this proposal is scoped from the founder's doc reading plus my own repo audit, not from reading the Cloudflare pages end to end.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · SERVICE-PROVENANCE-1 — the deploy-freshness stamp + the contracts declaration sweep
**Two record-integrity gaps, bundled because neither changes behaviour. Provenance failed TWICE this session — the deployed boutik producer was a version behind its repo, and shop-plus reported a merge never pushed to origin. Both were caught by reading bytes by hand. This closes the first class; nothing closes the second except discipline.**
- **THE STAMP:** `/health` now answers `release` (git sha — WHICH BUILD is running) and `canon` (the pinned `@platform/contracts` version — WHICH WIRE SHAPE it speaks), injected at BUNDLE time by esbuild `--define` from the deploy workflow. **`canon` is the field that matters, and the reasoning is the part to keep: a sha ALONE would NOT have caught the stale producer** — it would have shown an unfamiliar hash and told nobody the wire shape was out of date.
- **Mechanism:** `packages/observability/src/provenance.ts` reads bare identifiers behind `typeof` guards — exactly what `--define` substitutes — so a bundled build carries the real values and every unbundled context (local dev, CI, tests) falls back to `dev`. One change in the shared health door; zero call-site changes. The workflow reads `canon` from the **INSTALLED** `node_modules/@platform/contracts/package.json`, never a hand-typed constant, so it cannot drift from what was actually bundled.
- **THE PROOF THAT MATTERS — asserted against the real artifact, not the source:** `media-service/test/provenance-bundle.test.ts` runs the REAL `bundle:worker` script with the env the workflow sets and asserts the artifact contains the injected values AND that `__BOUTIK_RELEASE__`/`__BOUTIK_CANON__` are GONE (surviving placeholders would mean the deployed Worker answers `dev` forever — silently useless, the same shape as the defect this closes). **Proven to bite:** with `--define` removed from the bundle script the test FAILS naming the surviving placeholder; restored, 43/43 pass. A fallback-only test would have passed either way.
- **HONEST LIMITS (kept, per founder):** it is a STAMP, not a guarantee — it reports what the bundle was BUILT FROM, so it detects staleness and version skew but not a bundle altered after build; and **it cannot fire on its own** — something must READ it. It closes the "nobody could tell" half, not the "nobody looked" half. `dev` is not a failure; it is the honest answer for an unstamped build.
- **THE SWEEP:** the six package.jsons declaring `@platform/contracts` at `d7b27bb` → `642f849` (commerce-core, media-service, catalog-service, inventory-service, supplier-service, fulfillment-service). All 8 declarations now agree with the override and the lockfile. **Proven declaration-only: `pnpm install --no-frozen-lockfile` left the lockfile BYTE-UNCHANGED** — the resolved graph never moved, because the override was already forcing 642f849.
- **A GATE CAUGHT MY MISS, and it is worth recording:** I updated the two health tests I had grepped and the aggregate gate run failed on a THIRD (catalog-service). Four services asserted the exact `/health` body; all four updated. The per-package run I did first was not sufficient evidence — the full suite was.
- **NAMED, NOT SWEPT (outside the founder's scope, reported for his ruling):** `packages/commerce-core/package.json:23` declares `@platform/kernel-types` at `f23407c` while the override forces `642f849` — **the same bump-law violation class**, one remaining instance.
