Not swept: the order named the six *contracts* declarations, and expanding scope silently is its own failure. **The sweep therefore closes the contracts declarations, not the whole class.** By contrast `services/fulfillment-service/package.json:28` declaring `@platform/certification` at `f23407c` is **NOT** a violation — there is no override for `certification`, so the declaration IS the resolution: it is the intentional readiness-domain split, proven safe because the contracts src diff `d7b27bb→642f849` was `commerce.ts` only.
- **GAP — media-service has NO deploy workflow.** `wrangler.toml` exists and the bundle script now stamps, but nothing invokes a deploy for it; only `offer-deploy.yml` exists (and it is the only workflow carrying the provenance env). media-service therefore cannot deploy via CI today. Reported, not built.
- **Green:** typecheck 11/11 · observability 7 · media-service 43 · offer-service 64 · supplier-app 248 · **ALL GATES GREEN** (run-gates.sh exit 0).
- **DO NOT MERGE** — awaiting founder approval.
- **FOUNDER-ORDERED ADDITIONS (he re-scoped: "I am the one who scoped it and I scoped it too narrowly"):**
  1. **kernel-types sweep completed** — `packages/commerce-core/package.json:23` `f23407ce`→`642f849`. **Every override-governed declaration now agrees**: contracts 8×642f849, kernel-types 2×642f849, ui-tokens 2×a832df0 (all matching their overrides). The only remaining non-matching declaration is `fulfillment-service`'s `@platform/certification` at `f23407c`, which is CORRECT — there is no override for certification, so the declaration IS the resolution (the intentional readiness-domain split). **Proven declaration-only both ways: non-frozen install left the lockfile BYTE-UNCHANGED, cold frozen install passes.** The class is now closed for every package the override governs.
  2. **`media-deploy.yml` added** — media-service was merged with a `wrangler.toml` but NO workflow, i.e. a merged service that could not deploy through CI. Mirrors `offer-deploy.yml` exactly (verified step-for-step: both 10 steps, same order, `pnpm build` BEFORE the bundle — the trap that killed shop-plus's first deploy), with the same provenance stamp so **both services are checkable in one curl**. Manual `workflow_dispatch` only, gated on `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`. No write secret is set by it: this Worker has no write route yet.
- **UNPROVEN UNTIL `media-deploy` ACTUALLY RUNS (media-service has NEVER deployed — nothing local or in CI validates any of it):** the R2 binding and the `beurni-boutik-product-media` bucket name in `wrangler.toml`; whether that bucket exists on the account at all; first-deploy behaviour generally; and the deployed read route end-to-end against real R2. Miniflare binds R2 by its OWN test config, never that file. **One first-deploy hazard does NOT apply here:** media-service has no Durable Object, so there is no `[[migrations]]` block to get wrong — the thing that most needed care on offer-service's first deploy is simply absent.
- **Small gate lesson recorded:** `no-expo-token-leak` is `git ls-files`-driven, so a NEW file is not scanned until it is committed. `media-deploy.yml` was correctly absent from the scanned list while untracked, and covered on the next run once committed. A new workflow is not gate-covered by being written — only by being tracked.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · STANDING NOTE — GATE COVERAGE IS TRACKING-SHAPED, NOT WRITING-SHAPED
- **The property (founder ruling — journal as a standing note, not an anecdote):** `no-expo-token-leak` discovers its inputs with `git ls-files` (`scripts/gates/no-expo-token-leak.mjs:27`). **Any gate built on `ls-files` covers a file by virtue of it being TRACKED, not by it being WRITTEN.**
- **Why that is the dangerous direction:** a new file is unscanned during exactly the window when it is most likely to be wrong — while it is being written and iterated. The gate goes green, and the green means "the tracked set is clean", which is not the claim a reader assumes it makes.
- **Observed, not theorised:** `media-deploy.yml` was absent from the token-leak gate's scanned-file list on a full green run while untracked, and present on the next run once committed. Both states verified by reading the gate's own output.
- **Standing practice:** when a slice adds a NEW file that any `ls-files`-driven gate should cover — workflows, scripts, configs carrying secrets or tokens — **commit it, then re-run the gate, and confirm the filename appears in the gate's own output.** A gate run before `git add` is evidence about the previous state of the repo, not about the change under review.
- **Which gates this applies to:** any using `git ls-files` (today: `no-expo-token-leak`). Gates that walk directories directly (e.g. `check-mint-path-entropy.mjs`, which `readdirSync`-walks `apps/`, `packages/`, `services/`) do NOT have this property — they see untracked files too. **The two kinds are not interchangeable and the difference is invisible from the gate's name.**

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · MEDIA-UPLOAD-ROUTE-1 — the first route through which a byte can enter the bucket
- **THE FINDING IT CLOSES (founder, read from the DEPLOYED bundle):** the shipped media Worker had exactly two routes — `GET /media/{key}` and a health fallthrough. **There was no upload route at all:** the upload/validate/store path existed in `src/` but was never wired to the Worker's `fetch`. The service could read from a bucket nothing could ever write to. Another provenance-class finding, again caught by reading real bytes.
- **`POST /media`** — raw image bytes as the body, nothing else. No multipart, no JSON wrapper, no filename field. The declared `Content-Type` is IGNORED: the magic-byte sniff decides the real format and the stored content type is DERIVED from the bytes (asserted with a deliberately lying `Content-Type: application/json` on a PNG).
- **NO CALLER INPUT CAN SHAPE A KEY (founder ruling).** The handler passes the service exactly two things — bytes and clock — and `mintMediaKey()` is arity-zero, so there is no parameter through which a filename, a proposed key, a pv or a counter could reach the object name. The route reads no name at all, which is stronger than treating one as metadata. Asserted with a hostile request carrying `?key=media/pwned&name=hero.jpg`, `X-File-Name: supplier-founder-001/hero.jpg` and `X-Media-Key: media/attacker-chosen`: the minted ref is still exactly `media/{uuid}` and contains none of them — including no supplier id, so the wire out-guard still accepts it.
- **THE GATE — reused, not rewritten (founder ruling).** The implementation moved to a new shared package **`@boutik/service-auth`**; each service keeps a THIN adapter binding it to its OWN secret (`OFFER_WRITE_SECRET`, `MEDIA_WRITE_SECRET`), so the services stay **independently revocable** while the constant-time compare, the fail-closed rule and the single identical 401 exist exactly once. offer-service's exported surface is UNCHANGED by design — `worker/index.ts` and its e2e import the same names with the same signatures, which is what makes the extraction safe to do to a live gate. **Evidence it was safe: offer-service 64/64 still green, including the real-workerd combined-Worker auth e2e.**
- **The gate precedes routing and storage:** asserted that an unauthorised upload returns 401 with **zero objects written** to the store, that the no-key and wrong-key 401s are byte-identical, that an unset secret refuses even a correct-looking key, and that GET stays open (a bad-shape read 404s for its SHAPE, not for auth).
- **Response is the ref and its measured facts** — `{ref, contentType, width, height, byteLength}` — asserted to match no `/bucket|r2|beurni|cloudflare|account/i` and to have exactly those five keys. **No CORS**, asserted absent: this is called by an app, not a browser.
- **NEW SHARED-GATE TESTS (10)** lock the properties both services now inherit: fail-closed on unset/empty, exact-match-only, one identical 401 naming no cause, reads never gated, unknown methods default to WRITE not open.
- **OPERATIONAL FINDING worth more than the test that found it — HTTP TRIMS HEADER WHITESPACE.** A key SENT with surrounding whitespace is trimmed in transit and still matches (harmless). But a **SECRET STORED with a trailing space or newline** — trivially easy when pasting into `wrangler secret put` — keeps that whitespace in the env while the header loses it, so **the correct key 401s forever and the deploy looks broken**. Asserted in both directions so nobody loses an hour to it. My original test asserted the opposite of the platform's real behaviour; corrected to the truth rather than to what I assumed.
- **UNPROVEN, AND THIS IS THE POINT OF THE SLICE:** `env.BUCKET.put` has **NEVER executed against real R2**. Every store in every test is the in-memory fake or a hand-written R2-shaped stub, and miniflare binds R2 by its own config. **A green suite does not prove the real write.** The proof is the founder uploading a real photograph and opening the returned URL on his phone — nothing before that is evidence about R2.
- **Green:** typecheck 12/12 · service-auth 10 · media-service 56 · offer-service 64 · **ALL GATES GREEN** (exit 0, zero gate failures).
- **DO NOT MERGE** — awaiting founder approval.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · SUPPLIER-AUTHORING-1 (slice 1, PART 1 of 2) — the write seam + bundle-absence enforcement
- **Context:** founder direction — he will not use a terminal for product work. The curl smoke test is withdrawn; **the R2 proof moves into slice 2 as the first on-device photograph.** Slice 1 touches no bytes and no R2, so it is unaffected.
- **BUILT — the seam** (`apps/supplier-app/src/supply/service.ts`): the supplier app's FIRST outbound calls (it made none before — grep-verified). Port + `HttpSupplyService` posting the mirrored `CreateOfferCommand` to `POST /offers` with `X-Write-Key`. RN-safe: no `@platform/*` runtime import (Metro law); the command shape is mirrored locally and the SERVICE validates at its boundary. **`assets` deliberately absent** — this slice authors products with no photographs, so the wire receives the honest `assetRefs: []`.
- **THE CONSTRAINT THAT MATTERED MOST — unset resolves to NOTHING, never to demo.** `resolveSupplyService()` returns the real client or **`null`**. There is no demo branch and no import of one. **Construction, not configuration**, exactly as ordered.
- **Why, grounded rather than asserted:** shop-plus's own JOURNAL records two demo fallbacks that shipped bundled and masked — a hardcoded `AICHA_TRUST` trust block that would appear on ANY real store, and an `orderedProducts` path filling gaps from the entire `VITRINE_SEED` catalogue. Both were unreachable while the store was empty and would have detonated the moment it was not. **A fabrication path in the bundle is a fabrication waiting for a code path**, so the fix is absence, not selection. Note shop-plus's *storefront* resolver is the weaker shape (`return new DemoStorefrontService()` on unset — bundled and selected); boutik deliberately does not copy it.
- **ENFORCED BY A GATE, and proven to bite:** `scripts/gates/no-demo-in-app-graph.mjs` fails if any app-reachable file imports the demo module, if the demo sentinel appears in app source, or if the resolver ever falls back to `DemoSupplyService`. **Planted an import into `journey.ts` → gate FAILED exit 1 naming the file; restored → OK, 39 app sources scanned.** Wired into `run-gates.sh`.
- **DEMO ADAPTER** (`src/supply/demo.ts`) — records commands for tests, fabricates nothing back. Imported ONLY by tests; carries the sentinel the gate searches for.
- **FAILURE LEGIBILITY (founder requirement — the device error string is now the ONLY diagnostic anyone gets):** every failure path returns a readable reason, never a generic error and never a throw into the UI. A non-2xx surfaces **the status AND the service's own words** (asserted: a 401 reason contains both `401` and `unauthorized`); a network failure names the cause; an unreadable body is refused rather than treated as success. Asserted that no path throws.
- **DELIVERY:** `expo-preview.yml` now passes `EXPO_PUBLIC_OFFER_BASE` + `EXPO_PUBLIC_OFFER_WRITE_KEY` from repo secrets, inlined at bundle time exactly as `EXPO_PUBLIC_ROOT` already is. **Two new repo secrets needed** before a preview can write. The key ships inside the published bundle (founder-accepted, mirrors shop-plus): stops scanners, not a determined attacker, and is not per-supplier identity — HARD GATE, no supplier but the founder authors until real identity lands.
- **Green:** typecheck 12/12 · supplier-app **260** tests (29 files) · **ALL GATES GREEN** (exit 0, zero failures) including the new gate.
- **NOT BUILT — PART 2 of this slice, stated plainly rather than implied done:** the authoring SCREEN. Nothing in the app yet calls `resolveSupplyService()` — there is no form to enter a product name, price, commission and stock, no « publier » action, and no honest « non configuré » state rendered when the resolver returns null. **The seam is the foundation, not the feature**; on its own it changes nothing the founder can see on his phone.
- **DO NOT MERGE** — awaiting founder approval.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · SUPPLIER-AUTHORING-1 part 2 — the authoring core, the product code, and the three field rulings
- **THE THREE FIELD RULINGS (founder, after I reported before building):**
  1. **`supplyMode` = `SELLER_HELD`, no form field.** NOT a default I chose: `PLATFORM_OWNED` is B+9-gated (PackLab), so the gate leaves exactly one lawful value. **HARD GATE — it becomes a real form choice only when B+9 opens.**
  2. **`handlingClass` OMITTED entirely.** Canon marks it `.optional()` (`commerce.js:39`); a supplier cannot honestly state one, so the field is absent. **Omitting an optional field is not the same as inventing a value for it** — the line the founder endorsed.
  3. **`facts` = `{}`.** An empty record is legal, and no facts claimed beats facts invented. (I flagged that "empty" is also a choice; he accepted it as the honest one.)
- **`productCode` — DERIVED, VISIBLE, EDITABLE (founder option (a)) — AND THE FINDING THAT JUSTIFIES IT, which HE found and I had missed:** `catalog-service/src/product.ts:108` builds `stableSku: \`${draft.productCode}-${counter}\`` — **`productCode` is the stem of variant SKUs**, not decorative. That service is a health stub today (nothing runs it), which is why I discounted it, but it is a real consumer in code. **It is NOT buyer-facing:** `stableSku` lives on `VariantSchema` and appears nowhere on the supply projection or in the app (grep-verified, both). So an internal identifier, not a claim about the world — deriving it is not the fabrication class. **REVISIT THIS RULING IF `productCode`/`stableSku` ever becomes buyer-facing or is used to MATCH products across systems** — then a derived value becomes a claim.
- **THE DISAMBIGUATION, proposed and chosen rather than assumed (he sells pagne — names WILL repeat, and two products sharing a stem would collide their variant SKUs the day variants exist):** a **short random suffix from the OS CSPRNG**. Rejected alternatives, with reasons: a COUNTER needs durable cross-product state that resets on reinstall and diverges across devices (two phones both mint `PAGNE-1`); a FORM-LEVEL uniqueness CHECK needs the network at authoring time, breaking offline-first (law 7). The suffix is stateless, works offline, needs no coordination. Entropy is passed IN (pure + testable) and drawn from the app's proven `crypto.getRandomValues` path — **the mint-path gate now scans this too and reports 2 mint paths, zero `Math.random`.** Alphabet omits 0/O and 1/I: he reads these aloud off a cracked screen in sunlight.
- **THE AUTHORING CORE** (`src/supply/authoring.ts`) — pure: form values in, a command or a typed refusal out; the screen decides nothing. Refusals are **collected, not one-at-a-time** (a form that reveals objections one per attempt on 3G is a form he abandons). The category floor is checked locally BEFORE a round-trip *and* still re-checked server-side — the service stays the authority.
- **PUBLISH STATES — none looks like success without being one:** `not_configured` (the seam resolved to null) · `invalid` · `sending` · `published` · `refused` (the service's own words) · `failed` (status + body, or the network cause, passed through UNCHANGED). **This is precisely the shop-plus reseller-adapter failure refused one layer up:** its demo `create` cannot fail, so an unset secret yields a success toast and writes nothing anywhere, leaving no artifact to notice — fabricating a SUCCESS rather than data. Asserted: a null service returns `not_configured`, an invalid form never reaches the network, and nothing reaches `published` without a real 2xx decision.
- **MONEY through the ONE existing formatter** (`src/v2/money.ts` `formatF`) — extended, not duplicated. Asserted: the canon suffix from ui-tokens (never a bare F) and U+202F grouping, never an ASCII space.
- **Green:** typecheck 12/12 · supplier-app **282** tests (30 files, +22) · **ALL GATES GREEN** (exit 0, zero failures).
- **NOT BUILT — stated plainly rather than implied done:** the RN SCREEN itself. The core, the derivation, the states and the money rendering are built and proven; **no view renders them yet**, so nothing on the founder's phone has changed. The screen is a render layer over `publish()` + `buildCreateOffer()` and is the remaining work of part 2.
- **NOT CONFIRMED (and I will not claim it):** that `EXPO_PUBLIC_OFFER_BASE`/`_WRITE_KEY` reach the bundle. The workflow passes them; GitHub does not expose secret values; **only a published preview against the real screen proves it** — which is why that confirmation belongs after the screen exists, not now.
- **DO NOT MERGE** — awaiting founder approval.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · SUPPLIER-AUTHORING-1 part 2 (cont.) — THE SCREEN. The founder can now author a real product from his phone.
- **BUILT** (`apps/supplier-app/src/v2/publier.tsx`, `SPublier`): the render layer over `publish()` + `buildCreateOffer()`. Seven fields — nom · code produit · catégorie · zone · prix de base · commission revendeur · quantité — one primary action, and every publish state rendered. It decides nothing: validation, the command shape and the states all come from the pure core.
- **WIRED WHERE HE ACTUALLY WALKS.** `expo-preview.yml:64` defaults `EXPO_PUBLIC_ROOT` to `v2`, so his preview mounts **AppV2**, not the E1 `App.tsx`. The Produits tab's single « Lister un produit — gratuit » CTA already dispatched `OPEN_WIZ` → view `'add'`; **that view now renders `SPublier` instead of `S20Wizard`.** The machine action and the view id are UNCHANGED, so §4's transition table and the pixel property-diff table both still hold — only what renders moved.
- **CONSEQUENCE, STATED NOT BURIED:** the S20 five-step demo wizard is **no longer reachable from the app**. Its « publier » added a fabricated product to the seeded board and raised a toast — precisely the fabrication this slice exists to kill — so I did not keep a second entry point beside it (two « nouveau produit » buttons on one screen fails the 5-second test). `S20Wizard` still EXISTS in `screens2.tsx` and still ships in the bundle, unrouted; it belongs with the rest of the seeded board, and removing it is not this slice's scope. **Founder: say the word if you want it back for pixel review.**
- **« NON CONFIGURÉ » IS A CONDITION, NOT AN ERROR (founder ruling on the string).** Shown as a calm INFO banner **before he types**, not after he taps, and the primary action is disabled so he never walks into a dead end. Final string: **« Pas encore relié au service. Rien ne sera envoyé pour l'instant. »** I dropped my earlier draft (« … Votre fiche reste ici ») because **nothing persists a draft yet** — a back-tap unmounts the screen and loses the form, so that clause would have been a promise the app does not keep. Asserted: the string carries no blame word (`erreur|échec|impossible|désolé`), no draft-keeping claim, and does say plainly that nothing was sent, so it cannot read as success.
- **`productCode` — the founder's option (a), made visible in the literal sense.** The field fills **as he types the name** (`PAGNETISSE-7K2M`) — no hidden affordance, nothing stamped behind his back — and the suggestion **stops the moment he edits it**. Suffix entropy is drawn once per mount so the code is stable while he types. **If no CSPRNG is available, nothing is suggested at all** rather than a code from a weak source.
- **A FOURTH UNSTATABLE FIELD, FOUND WHILE WIRING — AND IT IS MINE TO FLAG, NOT TO CLOSE.** `OfferDraft` requires `effective`/`expiry`, and they are not decorative: `services/offer-service/src/projection.ts:83` refuses the read with `offer_not_effective` when `now > expiry`. **Past the expiry the product silently stops being served to Shop+ — a disappearance with no error anywhere.** A supplier cannot state when his product stops being for sale, so the window is DERIVED (`effective = now`, `expiry = now + 365 days`) and the screen STATES the consequence in plain words (« Cette offre reste en ligne un an. ») rather than hiding it. **365 IS MY NUMBER, NOT THE FOUNDER'S** — the safest of the available defaults (a short window fails invisibly; a long one is correctable) but an invented figure, and he must ratify or replace it. **HARD GATE: no edit path exists, so an offer published today cannot have its window changed from the app.** Asserted: the window spans the read-path check at the instant of publish, and refuses an unparseable clock rather than minting an offer that never serves.
- **THE SELLER NET IS THE SERVICE'S FIGURE OR IT IS ABSENT.** `previewSellerNet` runs `computeWaterfall` + `assertQuoteReconciles` server-side and the worker forwards it verbatim on a `created` decision; the screen renders **that** number through the existing `formatF`. **An idempotent re-tap carries no preview, so no figure is shown** — never a recomputed one, never a remembered one. Asserted both ways, and asserted that the screen calls neither `fee()` nor `net()`. **The app computes no money.**
- **NUMBER PAD ON THE THREE FCFA FIELDS** — `Input` gained an OPTIONAL `keyboardType` (no style change, property-diff table untouched). A franc field that opens the alphabetic keyboard on a 1GB Android is a failed screen.
- **A REAL DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED BEFORE IT SHIPPED:** `randomSuffixBytes()` draws from the ambient `crypto.getRandomValues`, which **React Native does not provide** — `commandId.ts` only surfaced `randomUUID`. On device the product-code suggestion would have thrown. `commandId.ts` now surfaces `getRandomValues` from expo-crypto through the same shape, in a SEPARATE block so the proven command-id path stays byte-identical. **No `Math.random` fallback**; mint-path-entropy still reports 2 mint paths, zero `Math.random`.
- **TESTS ARE RED-PROVEN, NOT ASSUMED.** The 15 new screen assertions are source-level (this suite has no RN renderer). I planted four defects — inline French copy in JSX, a catalog key that does not exist, a dropped `number-pad`, a missing reference — and **each failed exactly the test that claims to catch it (4 failed / 11 passed)**, then restored to green. Also asserted: every `t(...)` key the screen uses exists in the catalog (`t()` throws at runtime on a miss, so it must fail in CI first), and every one of the 8 `FieldError` members maps to a real catalog string.
- **Copy-lint:** 236 entries, 0 violations (+5 strings: validité, référence, net, réessayer, retour; `publier.non_configure` rewritten).
- **Green:** typecheck OK · supplier-app **302** tests (31 files, +20) · **ALL GATES GREEN** (exit 0, zero failures; `no-demo-in-app-graph` now scans 42 app sources including the new screen).
- **STILL NOT CONFIRMED, AND I WILL NOT CLAIM IT:** that `EXPO_PUBLIC_OFFER_BASE`/`_WRITE_KEY` reach the bundle. Only a published preview run against this screen proves it. **If the first publish 401s, the rotated secret / `EXPO_PUBLIC_OFFER_WRITE_KEY` mismatch is the first thing to check, not the code.**
- **DO NOT MERGE** — awaiting founder approval. Part 2 core + screen merge together, as ordered.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · SUPPLIER-AUTHORING-1 part 2 — TWO WRITE-PATH DEFECTS I FOUND IN MY OWN SCREEN, before review
Found on an adversarial re-read of `onPublish` after the first green. Both are on the live write path, so they are recorded rather than quietly patched.
- **DEFECT 1 — A RETRY COULD CREATE A SECOND PRODUCT.** The screen minted fresh ids on every tap, so « Réessayer » sent a **new `commandId`** — throwing away the exact key the service is idempotent on (`decideCreateOffer`: an existing entry with the same command id answers `idempotent` and writes nothing). **The scenario that bites:** the POST reaches the service, the offer IS created, and the response is lost on the way back (3G drops, tunnel, router dies). The screen honestly shows `failed`; he taps retry; with fresh ids that second request is a **new create** — two products, both his, indistinguishable. **FIX:** `retainIdentity()` in `authoring.ts` mints the three ids once per authoring attempt and hands back the SAME object forever. The CLOCK is deliberately NOT retained — `asOf` is a real supply-state write time, so each attempt carries its own; only identity must be stable. Asserted with a counting mint: a retry advances the counter by **zero** and the `commandId`/`offerId` are identical across three attempts.
- **DEFECT 2 — THE DOUBLE-TAP GUARD READ ASYNC STATE.** `if (state?.kind === 'sending') return` reads the render closure, and `setState` is asynchronous: on a stalled JS thread — the 1GB Android this is built for — two taps can both see the pre-render value and both fire. With defect 1 present that was two identities and two products. **FIX:** a `useRef` written synchronously, released on both exit paths (asserted: exactly two releases, or the button would be dead forever).
- **Both are source-asserted, not behaviourally tested, and I say so plainly:** this suite has no RN renderer, so the screen cannot be driven. `retainIdentity` itself IS behaviourally tested (it is pure); the screen's *use* of it is pinned by source assertions. **Red-proven:** reverting the guard to the state-based form and re-minting ids inline failed exactly the three tests that claim to catch them (3 failed / 14 passed), then restored to green.
- **KNOWN LIMITATION, STATED NOT FIXED:** backing out of the screen mid-send does **not** cancel the send. The request is already in flight; if the service accepts it the product exists and he will not have seen its reference (he can find it on the key-gated `GET /offers` admin list). Building a cancel would mean either trapping him behind a hung 3G request or lying about what a back-tap does; I did neither and am flagging it instead.
- **Green after the fix:** typecheck OK · supplier-app **307** tests (31 files) · **ALL GATES GREEN**.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · SUPPLIER-AUTHORING-1 part 2 — FRESH-CONTEXT VERIFIER: **FAIL**, and the repairs
The verifier was given only the quoted rules, the diff and the DoD. It returned FAIL with 15 findings — including one my own fix had **introduced**. Recording all of it; the ones I fixed and the ones I am handing back.

**FIXED — the write path and the money surface**
- **`idempotent` was rendered as a plain success while the live offer could hold OLD values.** Retaining the identity (my earlier fix) made `idempotent` reachable — and the form stays editable in the `failed` window. So: publish at 10 000 → response lost → he corrects to 12 000 → « Réessayer » → same `commandId` → the service answers `idempotent` **with the first entry** → the screen said « C'est publié » with no figure. He would believe 12 000 was live. `PublishState.published` now carries **`alreadyRegistered`**, and an idempotent answer renders its own calm sentence — « Ce produit était déjà enregistré. C'est le premier envoi qui est en ligne. » A money moment must not make him *wrong*.
- **`publier.echec` was FALSE on the two commonest failures.** It said « voici ce que le service a répondu » over a network failure (nothing was sent) and over a CSPRNG failure (no request was ever built). `ServiceResult` failures now carry a typed **`FailureCause`** (`network` · `http` · `unreadable` · `device`), only `http` may claim the service spoke, and **`network` is now a designed offline state** — « Pas de réseau. Rien n'a été envoyé. Réessayez quand la connexion revient. » in a calm tone, not a red wall with a raw English RN error in it.
- **The response boundary was unvalidated — on a money path.** `JSON.parse(text) as CreateOfferOutcome` is a compile-time lie: a 2xx body of `null` made `res.value.status` a TypeError thrown mid-render (there is no error boundary in this app), and a non-numeric `sellerNetFcfa` reached `formatF` and rendered a wrong figure **after** the offer was created. New `readOutcome()` validates by hand (zod is unavailable under the Metro law). A malformed preview **drops the figure and keeps the decision** — the offer really was created, so refusing the whole response would be the bigger lie. **No figure beats a wrong one.**
- **A throw would have latched « Envoi en cours… » forever.** My `inFlight` ref had no `finally`, so any throw left the primary action dead for the rest of the mount — the guard made recovery *worse* than no guard. Now `try/finally`.
- **`effective` came from the DEVICE clock and is judged against the SERVER clock.** The near end of the window bites with no year passing: a phone running a day fast stores an `effective` in the future, and every Shop+ pull refuses `offer_not_effective` while the screen has already said « c'est publié ». `effective` is now backdated by **`CLOCK_SKEW_ALLOWANCE_DAYS = 2`** — not a lie about when the offer starts, only an absorption of two clocks disagreeing about "now". The expiry keeps its full span. Asserted by driving the **real** `projection.ts:83` predicate, including a skew beyond the allowance still failing (the guard is bounded, not magic).
- **`commandId.ts`'s second block could destroy the first.** Separate in the SOURCE is not separate at RUNTIME — both write the same global. A spread copies own-enumerable properties only, so on a runtime whose `crypto` is non-extensible and already carries `randomUUID` on the PROTOTYPE (block 1 therefore skipped), the replacement would have dropped `randomUUID`, `mintCommandId()` would throw, and **publishing would be impossible**. Any existing `randomUUID` is now carried forward explicitly, bound to its receiver.
- **Two strings promised things the app cannot do.** « Vous pourrez en ajouter plus tard » (photos) — there is no edit path anywhere: `decideCreateOffer` answers `collision` for a second command and no update route exists. And « Retour aux produits » promised a list that **does not contain his product**. Now « Ce produit part sans photo. » and « Terminer ». Both pinned by tests that read the catalog.
- **A dead, self-duplicating branch** (`not_configured` rendered twice, unreachable because the button is disabled in exactly that case) — removed.
- **Two stale comments that this commit's own claim disproves** — `AppV2.tsx` and `index.ts` both said E1 was the default root. `expo-preview.yml:64` defaults to `v2`. JOURNAL:837 had flagged the `index.ts` line and deferred it "to the next app-code wave"; this is that wave. Corrected.
- **Two of my own tests asserted less than their names claimed** (failure mode 7, caught in my own new tests): the window test compared strings it had just built — a tautology that would survive `projection.ts` changing its rule — and the "ONLY from published" test asserted merely that an `if` existed. Both rewritten to drive the real predicate / pin the count and location. The inline-copy test's comment claimed it covered string props while the regex matched text nodes only; the regex now covers both, which is the likelier leak here since every string on this screen is a prop.

**CORRECTED FACTS I HAD STATED WRONGLY**
- I wrote "the Produits tab's **single** CTA". There are **TWO** entry points — `screens1.tsx:134` (Produits) and `screens1.tsx:83` (Accueil « Ajouter un produit »). Both dispatch `OPEN_WIZ`, so both now open the real screen.
- **The routing change also killed the Studio demo, which I did not state.** `OPEN_STUDIO` is dispatched *only* from inside `S20Wizard` (`screens2.tsx:175`), so `S26Studio` and the whole proof-photo demo are now unreachable, while `AppV2.tsx` still renders a `v.s === 'studio'` branch that cannot be entered. **FOUNDER DECISION, not mine** — this is a bigger loss than the wizard alone, and restoring both behind a secondary entry is a small change if he wants them for pixel review.

**HANDED BACK, NOT FIXED**
- **`asOf` is sourced from the phone.** It is the input to Shop+'s staleness gate (SW-2), and `offer-core.ts:38` calls it "real supply-state write time". This screen is the first client to source it from a device clock; a forward-skewed phone makes stale data look fresh at a gate that guards agreement. The right fix is for the SERVICE to stamp `asOf` from its own clock — an offer-service change, so it is his call, not mine.
- **The Produits board is still seeded fiction.** A real product now writes to the live service and then does not appear in the only "products" surface the app has. That is the next slice, and it is the whole point of « de-mock completely ».
- **A latent coupling:** `machine.ts:178` still runs wizard step-back for view `'add'`. Unreachable today (nothing sets `wiz.step > 0` now), but any future path that does would silently make the back arrow do nothing for three taps.
- **Backing out mid-send still does not cancel the send** (recorded earlier, unchanged).

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · BUNDLE-ABSENCE — the demo-adapter property is now a MEASUREMENT (cross-lane relay, founder order)
- **What changed:** `no-demo-in-app-graph.mjs` reasons over SOURCE — an inference about what Metro would do. **`scripts/gates/bundle-absence.mjs` runs `expo export` and greps the real Metro/Hermes artifact.** Same class of evidence as grepping a Worker bundle. Both gates stay: the source gate is fast and names the offending file; this one is the measurement. **~9s warm, ~20s cold; the whole gate suite is 27.7s.**
- **The fingerprint choice, on the reasoning the relay gave rather than by copying its strings:** the primary is a **string literal — data**, because a minifier may rename any class it likes but cannot delete a string the program still holds. `DemoSupplyService` is kept as a **secondary signal that reports but never fails alone** — the gate must fail on PRESENCE, not on naming fashion. I also made the sentinel **reachable from the class** (`DemoSupplyService.sentinel`) so it cannot be dropped as an unused binding while the adapter is in the graph.
- **A MEASURED CONSTRAINT NOBODY WOULD HAVE GUESSED: fingerprints must be ASCII.** Hermes stores non-ASCII literals as UTF-16 in its string table. In one and the same artifact, `Ce produit part sans photo` was found and `Pas encore relié au service` was **not**. An accented fingerprint would have made this gate pass vacuously forever.
- **Which is why the gate carries a POSITIVE CONTROL:** it asserts that two known ASCII literals from real app code ARE present, and exits **2** if they are not. If a future Hermes/Metro/minifier change made literals unreadable, the absence check would start passing for the wrong reason; the control turns that into a hard failure instead.
- **RED-PROVEN, and the first attempt was an invalid proof I caught myself.** I planted `return new DemoSupplyService()` without a working import: the gate stayed green and reported only the secondary signal — correct behaviour, but not a proof, because Metro never had the module in the graph. Re-planted with a real import: **exit 1, naming `BOUTIK_DEMO_SUPPLY_ADAPTER_MUST_NOT_SHIP` in the `.hbc`.** Restored: exit 0. The source gate stayed green throughout (42 sources) — it cannot see that class of plant, which is exactly why the measurement was worth building.
- **THE LIMIT, STATED RATHER THAN CLOSED:** `expo export` proves ONE platform's artifact, built from the same Metro graph and entry as the EAS build — **not the EAS artifact itself**. The service can prove what shipped; the app can prove what *should* ship. This narrows that gap a long way without erasing it.
- **Green:** typecheck OK · supplier-app **322** tests (31 files) · **ALL GATES GREEN** · copy-lint 240 entries, 0 violations.
- **DO NOT MERGE** — awaiting founder approval.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · SUPPLIER-AUTHORING-1 part 2 — MERGED. Release `1ee76b4`.
- Guarded merge of the approved `f47342a`: remote heads verified first, `--no-ff`, **tree diff vs the approved commit EMPTY**, re-proved green on the merge commit (typecheck · 322 supplier-app tests · ALL GATES GREEN), then pushed. **Ancestry read-back from the remote:** `f47342a` IS an ancestor of `origin/main`, and `git diff origin/main f47342a` is empty — what shipped is byte-for-byte what was approved.
- **FOUNDER RULINGS RECORDED:**
  - **The 365-day window is RATIFIED** — "the duration was never the problem, the silence was". **TWO REAL FIXES REQUIRED BEFORE REAL SUPPLY DEPENDS ON IT:** (1) the expiry must become **visible somewhere he can see it** — today it exists only in the command and a one-line French sentence on the authoring screen; (2) a **renewal path is required** — there is no edit route at all (`decideCreateOffer` answers `collision` for a second command against an existing offer), so an offer published today cannot have its window extended by any means. Both are open work, neither is built.
  - **DO NOT restore the Studio demo.** Its purpose was previewing a capture flow that slice C builds for real; restoring it is work that gets deleted. The dead `v.s === 'studio'` render branch stays until slice C, unreachable and harmless.
  - The seeded Produits board is the **next slice**, not a decision.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · EXPO_PUBLIC INLINING — measured, and a cache finding that would have wasted a day
- **The mechanism is PROVEN, by measurement rather than by reading babel docs.** Exported with `EXPO_PUBLIC_OFFER_BASE=https://PROBE-BASE-9F3K.example` and `EXPO_PUBLIC_OFFER_WRITE_KEY=PROBE-KEY-7QX2`, then grepped the Hermes artifact: **both probe strings PRESENT**, alongside the `supplier-founder-001` positive control. Dot access on `process.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_*` really is inlined into the shipped bundle.
- **THE FINDING: METRO'S TRANSFORM CACHE MASKS AN ENV CHANGE.** The FIRST probe run — same command, same values, warm cache — produced a bundle with **both probes ABSENT**. The env value is not part of the file content that keys the transform cache, so a previously-transformed module carrying the OLD (or unset) value is reused. `--clear` produced the correct bundle. **Anyone debugging "my secret didn't reach the app" will hit this and blame the secret.** CI is unaffected — each run gets a fresh runner and `actions/setup-node`'s cache covers the pnpm store, not Metro's temp-dir cache — but a local export is affected every time.
- **Why `bundle-absence.mjs` does NOT need `--clear`:** its property is about the module GRAPH and file contents, both of which key the cache correctly — empirically confirmed by the red-proof, which caught the planted import on a warm cache. Noted here so nobody "fixes" it by adding 11s to every run.
- **STILL NOT PROVEN, and it cannot be from here:** that the repo SECRETS are non-empty. GitHub never exposes secret values. **The screen itself is the one-glance test:** unset ⇒ `resolveSupplyService()` returns null ⇒ the primary action is disabled and « Pas encore relié au service » shows *before he types*. A live Publier button means both values reached the bundle.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · THE ASOF REVERSAL — the blocker that would have emptied every vitrine
- **FOUNDER CALL, and he was right that this was never mine to hand back.** I filed device-clock `asOf` as a founder decision; it is a blocker. He traced both sides and I verified both myself before touching anything:
  - Boutik served `asOf: entry.asOf` — the WRITE time, carried from the create command, sourced from his phone (`supply-endpoint.ts:131`, pre-change).
  - Shop+ blocks anything older than `SUPPLY_PROJECTION_MAX_AGE_MS` = **15 minutes** (`shop-plus/packages/supply-consumer/src/read-model.ts:19`, founder ruling 2026-07-15, = QUOTE_TTL_MS) — read in the shop-plus tree, not taken on trust.
  - **Consequence: a product authored at 10:00 served `asOf 10:00` forever. At 10:16 Shop+ refused it. EVERY REAL PRODUCT WOULD HAVE VANISHED FROM EVERY VITRINE FIFTEEN MINUTES AFTER CREATION — silently, on the buyer's side, with every gate green in both repos.**
- **BUILT: `serveProjection` stamps `asOf` from the SERVE clock** (`nowIso`, which it already received for the refusal ladder). One line; the reasoning around it is the artefact.
- **THIS REVERSES A DELIBERATE, TEST-PINNED DECISION.** The old test was named « asOf is TRUTHFUL — staleness is real age, never fabricated freshness ». That reasoning is right about a CACHED read and wrong about this one, and I verified the premise rather than repeating it: **nothing is memoised on this path** — `makeSupplyFetch` calls `store.getEntryByProductVersion` on every request and `buildSupplyProjection` recomputes each time. The value genuinely IS accurate as of `nowIso`. What the consumer's bound now catches is what it was always for: a RESPONSE that sat in an edge cache, a retry queue or a resumed offline request — asserted, so the reversal did not disarm SW-2.
- **A FALSE GREEN I CAUGHT.** `pnpm exec vitest run` reported 67/67 green with the e2e passing its OLD `asOf === T0` assertion. That is because `pnpm exec` does **not** run the `pretest` hook that rebundles the Worker — the miniflare e2e was testing a **stale bundle**. Run as `pnpm test`, the real workerd e2e failed exactly as it should. **A workerd e2e is only evidence about the bundle it was actually given.** The e2e now asserts the real property on real workerd: served `asOf` is not `T0`, is a real clock past it, and is fresh at the 15-minute bound.
- **`entry.asOf` AFTER THE CHANGE — the question the founder asked, answered honestly: it now has ZERO READERS.** Grep-verified across `src/` and `worker/`: it is written by `decideCreateOffer` and read nowhere; the admin list (`GET /offers`) does not expose it. **KEPT, not deleted** — it is the only record of when a supply state was authored and it is already persisted in live durable objects, so removing it would change the stored shape of existing offers to gain nothing. Its doc comment claimed "the endpoint returns it verbatim (truthful staleness)", which **is now false and has been corrected** rather than left to mislead. **HARD GATE: it must never become a decision input — it is device-sourced and therefore a record, not evidence.**
- **Green:** workspace typecheck 13/13 · offer-service **67** tests **via `pnpm test`, with the Worker rebundled** (incl. the real-workerd e2e) · **ALL GATES GREEN**.
- **DO NOT MERGE** — awaiting founder approval.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · TWO STANDING RULES, promoted from findings (founder order)
Both are provenance-family failures — the class where everything looks green and the truth is elsewhere. Recorded as rules, not anecdotes.

**RULE — A WORKERD E2E IS ONLY EVIDENCE ABOUT THE BUNDLE IT WAS ACTUALLY GIVEN.**
`pnpm exec vitest run` does **not** run the `pretest` hook. In this service `pretest` is `bundle:worker` — so `pnpm exec vitest run` runs the miniflare e2e against **whatever `dist/worker/worker.mjs` was lying on disk**, which may predate every change in the working tree. It reported **67/67 green with the e2e passing its OLD `asOf === T0` assertion** after I had already changed the code. Run as `pnpm test` it failed immediately, exactly as it should.
- **This is one layer deeper than a stale deploy or an unpushed merge: the TEST was the liar.** A false green from a real workerd e2e is the most expensive kind, because it is the thing everyone trusts — it is the evidence you reach for to disprove the other two.
- **THE RULE: always `pnpm test` for any service with a bundle step; never `pnpm exec vitest run`.** If you must scope to one file, bundle first. Treat "the e2e passed" as a claim about a bundle whose provenance you have checked.

**RULE — METRO'S TRANSFORM CACHE MASKS AN ENV CHANGE, AND IT HAS A SPECIFIC VICTIM.**
`EXPO_PUBLIC_*` values are inlined at transform time, but the env value is **not part of the cache key**, so a warm cache reuses a module carrying the OLD (or unset) value. Measured: the same `expo export`, same probe values, produced a bundle with **both probes ABSENT**; `--clear` produced one with both PRESENT.
- **The victim: anyone debugging "my secret didn't reach the app" will blame the secret and be wrong.** They will rotate a working secret, re-set it, and see no change.
- **THE RULE: any local `expo export` used as EVIDENCE about an env-derived value must pass `--clear`.** CI is unaffected (fresh runner per run). `bundle-absence.mjs` deliberately does not use `--clear` — its property is graph- and content-keyed, which the cache handles correctly, empirically confirmed by its red-proof catching a planted import on a warm cache.
- **FOUNDER-FACING:** if a preview ever behaves as though a value is missing, this is the first thing to rule out — before touching the secret.

### 2026-07-24 (cont.) · SLICE A · SUPPLY-READ-AUTH — the wire's read side is gated
- **READ THE CALLER'S SOURCE FIRST, as ordered — and it paid.** From shop-plus `services/storefront-service/src/supply-source.ts` on its `origin/main` (`b3b7c10`), not from prose: header **`Authorization`**, scheme **`Bearer `**, secret named **`SUPPLY_READ_SECRET`**, plus `Accept: application/json`, method GET, path `/supply-projection/{encodeURIComponent(pv)}`. My gate matches all of it, and a test asserts the constants **against shop's own file** so a rename on either side fails loudly instead of silently.
- **THE COUPLING THAT MAKES THIS DANGEROUS, and it is a gap I am reporting rather than closing:** shop's header is **env-gated on its side** — `HttpSupplySource.headers()` omits `Authorization` entirely when its `SUPPLY_READ_SECRET` is unset or empty. And shop treats **any non-2xx as `undefined` → the product is OMITTED**. So if the two secrets disagree, or shop's is unset, **there is no error anywhere: every product silently vanishes from every vitrine, with both repos green.** Same silent-disappearance class as the asOf bug. I cannot verify shop's Worker env from here — the founder states the same value is set on both. **The live probe after deploy is the only thing that proves it.**
- **BUILT on the SHARED module, not a second implementation** (founder ruling): `@boutik/service-auth` gains `BEARER_HEADER`/`BEARER_PREFIX`, `bearerTokenFrom`, `bearerAuthorizedAgainst`, `rejectUnauthorizedBearer` — reusing the SAME `timingSafeEqual` the write gate uses. offer-service keeps a thin adapter (`rejectUnauthorizedSupplyRead`) bound to its own `SUPPLY_READ_SECRET`, so the two secrets stay independently rotatable.
- **GATED BEFORE `resolveOfferStore`**, so an unauthorised caller never reaches a Durable Object and **the 401 cannot be an existence oracle**. Proven on real workerd both ways: without the secret a real pv and a nonsense pv are **byte-identical 401s**; with it they are 200 and 404 — so the gate, not a blanket refusal, is what hides the difference.
- **THE TWO CREDENTIALS ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE, asserted:** the app write key (which ships inside a downloadable bundle — scanners, not attackers) does not open the supply read, and a bearer does not open a write.
- **`/health` stays UNGATED, deliberately** — it is how the deploy is verified and it carries no supply data. Asserted, including on a Worker with no secrets at all.
- **A TEST THAT WAS RED FOR THE WRONG REASON, corrected to the platform's truth:** I asserted a trailing space in the bearer was refused; it is **accepted**, because HTTP trims header values. The assertion now matches reality and pins the asymmetry that actually bites — whitespace SENT is trimmed and still matches, whitespace STORED in the secret (trivially easy via `wrangler secret put`) stays in the env and **401s the correct secret forever**, which on this wire is a silently empty shop.
- **RED-PROVEN:** removing the gate call from the composition root fails exactly the 4 e2e assertions that claim to catch it (4 failed / 68 passed), then restored to 72/72.
- **`wrangler.toml` corrected** — its comment ended "GET /supply-projection stays open (the wire's read side)", now false. Both secrets documented with the never-in-`[vars]` rule and the silent-empty-vitrine failure mode.
- **Green:** workspace typecheck 13/13 · service-auth **23** tests · offer-service **72** tests **via `pnpm test`** (worker rebundled; real-workerd e2e included) · **ALL GATES GREEN**.
- **DEPLOY STILL HELD** as ordered — slice B rides the same deploy.
- **DO NOT MERGE** — awaiting founder approval.

### 2026-07-25 · NAMED FAILURE MODE — THE SILENT-DISAPPEARANCE FAMILY (founder order: write it down)
**Three instances in one day. All three: no error anywhere, both repos green, every gate passing, and the product simply gone from the buyer's side.**
1. **`asOf` as write time** — Shop+ blocks past 15 minutes, so every product vanished from every vitrine 15 minutes after creation.
2. **The ambiguous empty state** — a screen that could read as success while nothing had been written.
3. **THE SUPPLY-READ SECRET MISMATCH (this slice's).** shop-plus `HttpSupplySource.headers()` **omits `Authorization` entirely** when its `SUPPLY_READ_SECRET` is unset or empty, and `describe()` maps **any non-2xx to `undefined`**, which the join turns into an **omitted product**. So a secret that is unset on one side, or differs between the two, produces **no error, no log, no failing test** — the vitrine just empties.

**WHY THIS IS THE REASON THE POST-DEPLOY LIVE PROBE IS NOT CEREMONY.** Every instrument we own is blind to this class: unit tests pass (each half is correct alone), CI passes (it never crosses the wire), the deploy workflow goes green (it deployed successfully), and `/health` answers 200 (it is ungated and carries no supply data). **The only instrument that sees it is a real request to the live `/supply-projection` carrying the real secret.** A green workflow is evidence that a deploy happened, never that the wire works.

**THE STANDING RULE:** after any change to either half of a service-to-service wire, verify against the LIVE response, not the run. For this wire specifically: probe `/supply-projection/:pv` **with** the bearer (expect 200 + a serve-time `asOf`) and **without** it (expect 401).

### 2026-07-25 · SLICE B · OFFER DISCOVERY — the collection a reseller browses
- **WIRE SHAPE, founder-approved and relayed to the shop lane BEFORE building** (the whole lesson of the last mismatch, applied before rather than after): **`GET /supply-projections`** → `{ asOf, items: [ {version, asOf, value}, … ] }`, **each item a COMPLETE canon envelope**.
  - **The reasoning, not the shape, is what decided it:** shop's certified `consumeSupplyProjection` runs **per item, completely unchanged** — same schema, same 15-minute bound, same identity sweep, **no new parser on the consumer side**. The last wire disagreed on path, envelope AND freshness simultaneously; a shape needing no new parsing code is worth its one redundancy, and **the redundancy is the feature**: an item lifted out of the list is still independently verifiable.
  - **`version` is PER-OFFER** (`entry.offer.version`), so a single flat envelope is **not expressible**. Structural, not a preference — asserted (`Object.keys(collection)` is exactly `['asOf','items']`).
  - **The outer `asOf` is kept** (founder ruling): one freshness decision about the whole response without iterating, and free because every item shares the serve clock by construction — asserted (`item.asOf === collection.asOf` for every item).
  - **ROUTE NAME IS PART OF THE CONTRACT AND OZ1 MUST MATCH IT: `/supply-projections`** (plural), beside the singular `/supply-projection/:pv`.
- **HOW THE REFUSAL LADDER IS ENFORCED — the founder's order improved on, with his agreement.** He said reuse `buildSupplyProjection`; the right level is **one above it**. `serveProjections` is a **FILTER over `serveProjection`** — the very function the single read calls — keeping only `ok: true`. That inherits the ladder (*product active · approved · offer active · effective*) **plus `assertServableValue`** (the strict canon parse and the supplier-identity out-guard) in one call. **The collection cannot reach around the single read: there is one function that decides what is servable, and this is a filter over its answers.** Any check added later is inherited with no second site to remember. Asserted byte-for-byte: `collection.items[0]` **equals** the single read's body for the same entry.
- **A REFUSED ENTRY IS OMITTED, NEVER REPORTED WITH ITS REASON.** Per-item refusal reasons in a browse response would tell an authenticated caller which product version ids exist but are unapproved — an existence signal the single read's gate exists to keep back. Asserted: no reason string, and no id of a refused product, appears anywhere in the payload.
- **FRESHNESS IS INHERITED, NO SECOND CONCEPT** — and the corollary is the part worth keeping: the effectivity check uses the **same clock** as the envelope, so an offer expiring between two reads simply **drops out of the next one**. Envelope freshness and offer effectivity are keyed to one instant; a second freshness notion would invent a distinction the code does not have. Asserted at 11:59 (present) and 12:01 (absent) around a 12:00 expiry, with a second offer still present so it is not a blanket empty.
- **THE COLLECTION IS GATED, AND IT NEEDED ITS OWN MATCH.** `/supply-projections` does **not** start with `/supply-projection/`, so the slice-A prefix check did not cover it — and getting that wrong **fails OPEN**, handing every servable offer's `basePrice` and `resellerCommission` to an unauthenticated caller in one request with no guessing at all. `isSupplyRoute()` now matches both, asserted on real workerd.
- **`listEntries()` returns RAW entries and judges nothing** — a store that pre-filtered would be a second place for eligibility to live and drift. The DO router gained `GET /supply-entries` (the collection analogue of `/supply-entry/:pv`), which honestly **skips orphaned index rows** exactly as the admin list does, so one dead offer cannot fail the whole collection.
- **PAGINATION CEILING, JOURNALED RATHER THAN BUILT (founder order):** the route returns **everything**, unbounded. At today's scale — one supplier, a handful of offers — a cursor would be speculative flexibility now and a permanent API obligation forever. **The real ceiling is the single-object index DO** (`INDEX_KEY`, one `IndexRow[]`), already journaled as "irrelevant at this scale, not infinite". **Revisit when either is true: the index approaches ~1 000 rows, or the collection response exceeds ~1 MB.** Both are far away and neither is silent — the response size is observable.
- **A GATE FIRED ON MY PROSE AND IT WAS RIGHT.** `no-seller-debit` caught the word "withhold" in a comment about existence signals. Reworded to "keep back"; **the gate was not weakened** — same ruling as the earlier "withdrawn"→"revoked".
- **RED-PROVEN TWICE, and the FIRST PROOF WAS INVALID AND I CAUGHT IT.** My first plant kept calling `buildSupplyProjection` with a frozen clock — so it was caught by the FRESHNESS tests, not the ladder ones, because the ladder was still being applied. That would have been a false proof of ladder inheritance. Re-planted a **true bypass** (emit every entry, ladder never consulted): **all three ladder assertions fail plus both freshness ones (5 failed / 84 passed)**, then restored to 89/89. Separately, dropping the plural from the auth match fails both collection-gate assertions.
- **Green:** workspace typecheck 13/13 · offer-service **89** tests **via `pnpm test`** (worker rebundled; real-workerd e2e included) · **ALL GATES GREEN**.
- **DEPLOY STILL HELD** — one deploy carries asOf + slice A + slice B, then `/health` read-back, then the live `/supply-projection` probe.
- **DO NOT MERGE** — awaiting founder approval.

### 2026-07-25 · NAMED HAZARD — A NEW ROUTE BESIDE A GATED ONE (founder order: journal it)
**When a new route is added near a gated one, THE GATE'S MATCH IS A THING TO RE-DERIVE, NEVER TO ASSUME.**
- The instance: slice A gated `SUPPLY_PREFIX = '/supply-projection/'` (**with a trailing slash**). Slice B added `'/supply-projections'`. The plural **does not match the singular's prefix** — so the existing check silently did not cover the new route.
- **THE COST IS ASYMMETRIC AND THAT IS THE POINT.** Getting this wrong **fails OPEN**, and the new route carries **more** than the one it sits beside: the single read leaks one supplier's cost structure to whoever guesses a product version id; the collection hands over **every servable offer's `basePrice` and `resellerCommission` in one unauthenticated request, with no guessing at all.** A route that carries more than its neighbour, added under an auth check written for a narrower shape.
- **The general shape:** an auth predicate is written against the routes that existed when it was written. Adding a route does not extend it. Neither does a similar-looking name — similarity is what makes it feel covered.
- **The move:** `isSupplyRoute()` now names both routes explicitly, and the e2e asserts the 401 on the collection specifically, so a future third route fails loudly rather than quietly opening.

### 2026-07-25 · TECHNIQUE — WHEN A FROZEN BEHAVIOUR IS WRONG ON A NEW PATH, MAKE THE PATH NOT REACH IT
Founder ruling, and worth naming as a general move rather than a one-off. `machine.ts:258` carries a **§9 FROZEN** rule: an empty product name becomes « Robe brodée bogolan ». Harmless on the seeded board; through a **real write** it would publish a real product under an invented name.
- **The answer is NOT to edit the frozen rule.** It is to block continue on an empty name so the machine **never reaches** the fallback. The rule stays literally intact and the fabricated name becomes unreachable on the real path.
- **Same family as `absent-from-the-bundle` rather than `unselected`** (the demo supply adapter) and as `unset resolves to null` rather than to demo: **prefer making a wrong thing unreachable over making it different.** Unreachability is provable and does not disturb what other things depend on.
- **ACHIEVABILITY — confirmed, with the one caveat stated.** `WIZ_NEXT` at `machine.ts:251-253` already refuses to advance when `disabled.wizContinue(s)` is true, and the publish branch is only reached from `step === 4`. Adding an empty-name condition to that predicate makes the fallback unreachable **through the wizard's own footer**, which is the only dispatcher of `WIZ_NEXT` in the app (grep-verified). **CAVEAT, stated rather than buried:** the guard lives in the reducer, so it holds for any dispatcher; but the fallback line itself remains in the publish branch, so a FUTURE action that jumps straight to `step: 4` without passing the predicate would reach it again. That is why the real write must also refuse an empty name at the core (`buildCreateOffer` already returns `name_required`) — **two independent refusals, neither of which edits the frozen rule.**

### 2026-07-25 · `zone` IS A SUPPLIER CONSTANT, NOT A PRODUCT FIELD (founder-approved)
Canon §5 puts `zone` on `Product/Version` as part of **`Location {pin, zone, landmark, directions, maskedRelay}`**, and it feeds **`DeliveryFeeQuote{zoneFrom, zoneTo, …}`** — so it is **where the supplier IS**, not an attribute of the product. Asking the founder the same answer once per product is friction for nothing. Also verified: `zone` is **not** among the seven canon supply-projection fields, so it never reaches Shop+ today.
- **It becomes a constant beside `SUPPLIER_ID`, with the same HARD GATE: a second supplier — or one supplier with two locations — makes it a real field.**

### 2026-07-25 · STANDING RULE — A PROOF ABOUT CALL STRUCTURE CANNOT CATCH A DEFECT ABOUT ARGUMENTS
Founder order, from the crop-space defect: *"the reason your red-proof missed it is the lesson worth more than the fix: your assertion checked WHICH FUNCTIONS WERE CALLED, not WHICH DIMENSIONS WERE FED IN."* Journaled as a standing rule because **it generalises well past geometry.**

**The rule.** A test that asserts a call happened proves the *wiring*. It says nothing about the *values that flowed through it*. Every defect where the right function is called with the wrong argument passes such a test silently — and argument defects are the common kind on this project, because the code is mostly correct functions composed by hand.

**Why it bit.** The crop test asserted that `renderCropDerivative` was called with `heroSquareCrop(...)`. It was. The defect was that `heroSquareCrop` received the DERIVATIVE's dimensions and the rect was applied to the MASTER — two different coordinate spaces, both present in scope, the wrong one passed. The assertion could not see the difference because it never looked at a number.

**What this obliges, from now on:**
1. **A red proof must plant a defect that changes a VALUE, and the test must fail on that value.** If the planted defect only changes which symbol appears in the source, the proof is about structure and does not license a claim about correctness.
2. **Where the value is unreachable from the test harness** (no RN renderer here, so no screen-level franc assertion), say so *in the test file*, assert the strongest available substitute, and **label it as a substitute** — do not let a structural assertion sit in the suite wearing the costume of a behavioural one.
3. **A capability-absence check is an honest substitute and a weak one.** Asserting a module holds no binding to the wrong function makes the defect impossible to express without a visible import diff. It is not a value proof. Name it as what it is.
4. **This rule audits existing gates too, and several assert shape rather than substance.** Named for future work, not silently carried: the source-regex suites in `test/authoring-screen.test.ts` and `test/df2-device-review.test.ts` assert structure by construction. The durable fix is an RN renderer harness; until then their headers state the limit.

### 2026-07-25 · THE ROUNDING RULING, APPLIED — the real flow FLOORS; frozen §3.4 untouched
Founder ruling (neither option I offered): *"Do not edit frozen §3.4, and do not remove the seller-net preview. The real flow computes its preview through THE CANON-CORRECT ROUNDING."*

**FIRST, THE VERIFICATION HE ORDERED — `previewSellerNet` DOES implement the canon law correctly.** Read, not assumed (`services/offer-service/src/offer.ts:40`): it computes nothing itself; it calls `computeWaterfall` and `assertQuoteReconciles` from pinned `@platform/contracts`, and returns the waterfall's own `sellerNet`/`sellerPlatformFee`. Canon's `rounding-law.js` implements `sellerPlatformFee = floor(B×5/100)` in **exact integer arithmetic** (it refuses to multiply FCFA by 0.05 in floating point at all) and defines the nets **by subtraction**, never as independent multiplications. No divergence to report there.

**BUT IT IS NOT REACHABLE FROM THE APP, AND THAT IS THE GAP.** `previewSellerNet` lives in a Cloudflare Worker service package; the Expo app does not and cannot depend on `@boutik/offer-service`. So the ruling is satisfied by a second **CALL SITE**, not a second implementation: `apps/supplier-app/src/supply/preview.ts` calls the SAME pinned canon functions. **The law keeps exactly one home** — `@platform/contracts` money/rounding-law — and two thin callers.
- **`v2/money.ts` §3.4 IS UNTOUCHED** (asserted by a test that pins both frozen lines verbatim). **CORRECTED after the verifier run:** an earlier draft of this line said it still serves "seed products, the machine's board-write, the Argent screens" — wrong. `fee`/`net` now have exactly ONE consumer left, `seed.ts`. `machine.ts` imports them but uses only `formatF` (a dead import, pre-existing, left alone rather than tidied unasked); `screens1.tsx` never imported them.
- **The `money` prop on `S20Wizard` is REQUIRED, not optional.** An optional prop defaulting to the demo math means a future caller who forgets it silently falls back to `Math.round` **on a money screen** — the silent-divergence family. Required means that caller fails to compile. `fee`/`net` are no longer imported into `screens2.tsx` at all: the capability to compute non-canon money is gone from the file, not merely unused.
- **THE CHANGE IS INVISIBLE TODAY AND THAT IS THE HONEST FRAMING.** The stepper reaches only multiples of 500, where `round(0.05·B) === floor(0.05·B)`. **No figure the founder can currently see moves by one franc.** What is removed is the LATENT divergence: the day the price grid changes, his preview does not quietly start rounding a franc toward the platform.
- **The pinning test is KEPT** (founder: "keep your pinning test either way") with its now-stale paragraph corrected — it claimed the wizard uses the demo math, which this change makes false. It still guards the board-vs-real-flow agreement.
- **RED-PROVEN, on a VALUE, per the standing rule above:** planting `Math.round` into `preview.ts` fails two tests naming the exact franc — `B=10010: expected 501 to be 500`. Restored → 9/9 green. The off-grid case is the one that bites; the on-grid case is asserted too, so the "invisible today" claim is measured rather than argued.
- **MEASURED IN THE REAL ARTIFACT:** `expo export` writes a 2.91 MB Hermes bundle and the canon money law is IN it — four ASCII data fingerprints from `rounding-law.js` probed, all present. One (`must be an integer FCFA amount, got`) is now a **permanent positive control** on the bundle-absence gate, red-proven (mutated → exit 2, naming the string). **What it proves: canon's money module ships. What it does NOT prove: which screen calls it** — that is the value test's job, and the control says so in its own comment.

### 2026-07-25 · FINDING — `@platform/contracts` IS A PHANTOM DEPENDENCY OF THE SUPPLIER APP
Discovered while applying the rounding ruling, **reported rather than filled** (standing order), because the fix is a lockfile change and the founder is about to run the preview publish that produces his R2 proof — the wrong moment to disturb install determinism.
- **The fact:** `apps/supplier-app/package.json` does **not** list `@platform/contracts`, yet `App.tsx:5` runtime-imports it (and `test/runtime-imports.test.ts` *requires* that import to exist). It resolves only because the ROOT `package.json` declares it and Node/Metro walk up to the root `node_modules`. `apps/supplier-app/node_modules/@platform/` contains only `i18n` and `ui-tokens`.
- **It is an anomaly, not the repo's norm — checked:** all six services declare `@platform/contracts` in their own `package.json`. The app is the only package that imports it undeclared.
- **It works today, and that is measured, not assumed:** the `expo export` in the bundle-absence gate resolves it and the canon money strings are present in the Hermes artifact. Every shipped preview since E1 has carried it.
- **Why it matters, at its TRUE size (verifier correction):** the risk profile is **unchanged by this diff, not raised by it**. `App.tsx:5` already module-imports `@platform/contracts` and `test/runtime-imports.test.ts` *requires* that import — the app could not bundle or boot without the resolution before this change. What is new is only that the seller's net figure is now among the things riding on it. A phantom dep holds as long as the root keeps the declaration and the installer keeps hoisting; neither is a guarantee the app itself makes.

The override means the resolved instance does not change. **Best done AFTER the R2 proof run, not before it.**

The suite's discriminating power rests entirely on the off-grid case, the remainder-class loop, and the throw assertions. Worth knowing precisely, rather than believing "the tests would catch it."
- **MEDIUM — the no-try/catch argument named the wrong enforcement point, and I repaired the claim.** I wrote that the steppers are bounded "(machine.ts `disabled.wizB`/`wizC`)". The predicates are *declared* in machine.ts; they are *enforced* only by two `onPress` handlers in screens2.tsx. The reducer's `WIZ_SET` is a plain spread with no validation, and the app has **no error boundary** (grepped: zero hits). So the guarantee belongs to the current dispatchers, not the state machine. **THIS DIFF CONVERTS THE FAILURE MODE:** a negative B used to render a wrong number; now it throws in render, which is a blank screen mid-listing. Currently unreachable — the verifier could not construct a path, nor could I. **Comment repaired to say exactly this; a catch was still refused** (swallowing a money-law violation hides the one thing the call exists to surface). **⏳ FOUNDER — flagged as its own decision, below.**

Renamed to `[source-text check]` with the distinction stated at the site. The file header was also overbroad — it claimed the whole file asserts numbers, while the second `describe` is entirely source-text. **Header now labels the two describes apart instead of averaging them into one claim.** Same family as the standing red-proof rule: the failure was letting a structural assertion pass as a behavioural one.
- **MEDIUM — a tautological assertion, replaced.** `expect(f).toBe(Math.floor((B*5)/100))` re-typed canon's own arithmetic and would pass for that reason. Replaced with the two bounds that *characterise* floor independently: `f*100 <= B*5` and `(f+1)*100 > B*5` — f is the largest integer whose fee does not exceed 5% of B. `Math.round` fails the upper bound wherever the fraction is >= .5.
- **LOW — three comments claiming more than the code honours, all repaired:** the "seed, machine, screens1" consumer list (only `seed.ts` consumes `fee`/`net`); the bundle control's "the app's money code is canon's, not a local re-implementation" (the frozen `Math.round` ships in the same bundle via seed.ts, and is supposed to); and the phantom-dep entry's risk framing (the resolution was already load-bearing before this diff — the profile is unchanged, not raised).

### 2026-07-25 · THE COMMISSION AXIS — BOTH REFUSALS (founder ruling)
Founder ruling on the gap the verifier found: *"on the B axis the core refused independently, so the screen was the second line of defence. Here there is NO second line… So: BOTH REFUSALS, not one."* And explicitly: **"non-positive, not negative. A net of exactly zero is as meaningless to publish as minus fifty and it would slip through a strictly-negative test."**

**THE REFUSAL REASON — proposed from the existing vocabulary, as ordered:** `commission_leaves_no_net`.
- The `FieldError` union's shape is `{field}_{condition}`; this sits on the same `commission_` prefix as `commission_invalid`. It names the **consequence**, not a threshold, because **there is no fixed ceiling** — a commission that is harmless at B = 50 000 ruins the sale at B = 5 000. `commission_too_high` would imply a ceiling that does not exist.

**THE ONE NEW STRING — proposed, lint-clean, and it is the founder's to approve or replace:**
- key `publier.err_commission_net` · register `money` · screenClass `status`
- « **Avec cette commission, il ne vous reste rien sur la vente. Baissez-la, ou augmentez le prix de base.** »
- **copy-lint: 249 entries, 0 violations.** Money register, plain, ~6th grade, no administrative French, no banned token. It states the CAUSE and then the TWO ways out — its sibling `publier.err_prix_plancher` states only the rule, and the founder's own trust test asks for what-happens-next. **The diff is six lines; no reformatting of the catalog.**

**HOW IT IS BUILT — one law, one home, two enforcement points:**
- **`netLineRefusal(B, C)` in `authoring.ts`** is the single predicate behind both refusals: below the floor, or a NON-POSITIVE net (`<= 0`). The net comes from the CANON waterfall, so the threshold is applied to the same franc the seller is shown.
- **The screen** calls it and renders the refusal; **`buildCreateOffer` calls the same function** so the core refuses with no screen involved. That is not a duplicated rule — it is the price-floor pattern (a shared constant, two enforcement points) that the founder has already ratified twice.
- **`money` became a UNION, `SellerNetLine`,** replacing `SellerPreview | null`. Null carried the absence, which was enough while ONE rule could cause it. Two rules with different words means **the reason must travel beside the absence** rather than be inferred at the render site — the screen now states the key it is handed and hardcodes no refusal of its own (asserted).
- **The reason maps through the EXISTING `ERROR_KEY` table**, which is `Record<FieldError, string>` — so adding the member **forced** the new mapping at compile time. No second mapping, no invented shape.
- **ONE CAUSE IS NEVER REPORTED TWICE:** at B = 500 both rules are true; the floor wins and the net rule does not also fire — the price is what he must fix. Asserted.
- **RED-PROVEN, three ways, each naming the founder's own words:** slipping `<= 0` to `< 0` fails two tests at the exact zero-crossings (C = 4 750 and C = 9 500) — *the defect he predicted*; removing the core's refusal fails three tests, restoring the single-line-of-defence state; the shape and wiring pins fail on any drift.
- **A GATE CAUGHT MY PROSE FOR THE THIRD TIME TODAY, and again it was right.** `no-seller-debit` fired on « fine » next to money — which in the money sense means a penalty (B+I-12: seller consequences are access-based, never money). Reworded to « harmless » / « passes ». **The gate reads my words as carefully as the product's, three times in one session; that is the gate working, not the gate being noisy.**
- **Green:** typecheck 13/13 · supplier-app **359** tests (33 files) · ALL GATES GREEN · copy-lint 249 entries, 0 violations.

- **Its judgement on the shared predicate, which I had flagged as a possible objection:** sharing does NOT defeat "the core refuses independently" — the ruling's failure mode was a *missing enforcement point*, not a duplicated rule, and duplicating the arithmetic would reintroduce the drift the price-floor pattern exists to prevent. The honest cost is that one bug fails both points, which is why the `<=`→`<` mutation going red is the load-bearing evidence.

- **LOW — a comment stated a compiler guarantee that does not exist.** I wrote that TypeScript narrows the union "only from the direct comparison". **False, measured by the verifier with `tsc --strict`:** TS 4.4+ aliased-condition narrowing handles the boolean alias. The code was correct; my stated REASON was not. Rewritten to the honest reason (readability at the point of use, plus a third union case forcing every direct comparison to be revisited). Note this false claim also existed on `origin/main` in its `money === null` form — I rewrote the sentence in the floor slice without checking it.

Listed in full. **And the `Number.isSafeInteger` guard is now explicitly named as NOT an overflow guard** — canon throws above ~`MAX_SAFE_INTEGER / 5`, which this does not pre-empt; ~1.8e13 taps away, pre-existing, but the guard should not read as wider cover than it gives.

> « Avec cette commission, il ne vous reste rien sur la vente. Baissez-la, ou augmentez le prix de base. »

**No change.** Ruled 2026-07-25. **The reasoning, recorded as ordered:** its sibling `publier.err_prix_plancher` states a rule with ONE fix; **this one has TWO — lower the commission or raise the base price — and he cannot infer them from the rule alone.** Stating the cause and then both ways out is what the trust test asks for, and is why this string is longer than its sibling rather than matching it.

### 2026-07-25 · COUNTING CORRECTION — one proof, not two (founder)
The `every FieldError maps to a real catalog string` repair added **two** checks; only one earns its place.
- **REDUNDANT with the compiler:** the union-versus-map half. `ERROR_KEY` is `Record<FieldError, string>`, so TypeScript **already** forces exhaustiveness — I said exactly that in my own report and then asserted it again anyway. Its real and much narrower value is **pinning that guarantee if anyone ever loosens the annotation** (to `Partial<>`, or to a plain object literal). Kept on those terms, not as coverage.
- **LOAD-BEARING:** that every mapped VALUE resolves in the catalog. The compiler cannot check a string against the i18n data, and **that was the actual defect** — a typo'd key passing while `t()` would throw at render.
- **The general form, worth keeping:** when a repair produces two assertions, ask which one the compiler already makes. Redundant assertions are not free — they make a suite look stronger than it is, which is the same family as the tautology and the cached freshness check.

**The price floor is refused in THREE places; the commission axis in TWO.** Verified in the service source at this release, not assumed:

- **MEASURED, not argued:** `computeWaterfall({B:5000, C:4800})` → `sellerNet = -50`, and **`assertQuoteReconciles` PASSES it.** Reconciling is not the same as positive — the identities hold perfectly for a negative net. The floor check also passes, because B is exactly at the floor.
